				purdue levels are industrial control system (ics) design methods for security also known as pera or purdue enterprise refrence architecture
				has many levels
				include streamlines network designs, management, and communication among teams, improve security and resillience of industrial operations
				targets (iec 62443)
					defense-in-depth
						the model's layered architecture creates multiple security checkpoints, making it more challenging for cyber threats to infiltrate critical systems

					risk mitigation
						the isolation of critical components minimizes the potential for unauthorized access and accidental damage, safeguarding operational integrity

					enhanced visibility
						the clear segmentation provided by the model facilitates comprehensive monitoring and threat detection, enabling proactive incident response

					regulatory compliance
						the alignment with industry skndards like iec 62443 ensures adherence to best practices and regulatory requirements

					the purdue model's compatibility with industry standards like iec 62443 fosters adherence to best practices and regulatory requirements.

					the synergy with industry standards like iec 62443 promotes adherence to best practices and regulatory requirements

				evolve attack vector like apt (advance persistent threat) or ics  , malware, ransomware ...

				levels
					level 0: physical process
						the foundational layer includes physical processes and equipment. these equipment are sensors, actuators, and field devices that directly interact with the physical world.

					level 1: basic control
						this layer houses controllers and programmable logic controllers (plcs) responsible for automating individual processes by translating sensor data into actionable commands.

					level 2: supervisory control
						this layer includes scada systems and human-machine interfaces (hmis), which aggregate data from controllers for process monitoring and control.

					level 3: manufacturing operations
						this layer comprises manufacturing execution systems (mes) and historians for managing and optimizing production processes, bridging enterprise systems and the shop floor.

					demilitarized zone (dmz)
						the dmz acts as a secure buffer between the ics network and external networks, housing security devices like firewalls and intrusion prevention systems (ips).

					level 4: enterprise network
						the enterprise network layer encompasses the broader it infrastructure, including business applications and internet connectivity.

				key technologies for enhanced ics security within the purdue model framework
					robust network security tools
						ngfw
						ips
						vpn
						siem (security information and event management)
							analys security logs
							central visibility
							facilitationg incident response

					effective endpoint security measures
						antivirus and anti-malware
						endpoint detection and response (edr)
							advanced solutions that utilize behavioral analytics and machine learning to detect and respond to zero-day threats and sophisticated attacks

						host-based intrusion prevention systems (hips)
							systems that proactively monitor system activity andblock malicious actions in real-time

						application whitelisting: restricting the execution of unauthorized applications to prevent malicious code from running on critical systems

					enhanced access control and authentication
						multi-factor authentication (mfa)
						role-based access control (rbac)
							assigns permissions and access privileges based on user roles and responsibilities, limiting access to sensitive data and systems

						privilege access management (pam
							controls and monitors privileged accounts, minimizing the risk of misuse and abuse of access

					efficient patch management and segmentation
						vulnerability management solutions
							tools that scan for vulnerabilities and prioritize remediation efforts, keeping systems up-to-date and protected against known exploits

						network segmentation
							utilizing vlans, firewalls, or software-defined networking (sdn) to create isolated zones within the network, limiting the lateral movement of threats

						microsegmentation
							applies granular access controls based on individual workloads and applications,providing better isolation and protection

					continuous monitoring and incident response
						security orchestration, automation, and response (soar)
							soar platforms automate incident response workflows, enabling faster and more efficient threat dntainment

						threat intelligence
							leverages threat intelligence feeds to identify emerging threats and proactively update security policies and controls

						security awareness training
							educates employees about security best practices and the importance of adhering to security policies

				operational technologies (OT)
					it/ot governance knowledge about you assets and resources

					ot vision is safety and  business continuity where fortinet define a ecosystem with fortinet products to create a fabric and manage all systems

				fortigate  > system > feature visibility > ot
					we can see effects on security fabric > asset identity center  > ot view
					fortigate and fortiswitch workes on level 2 another devices works on level 3 except level s , 0 and external

				diagnose user-device-store device memory ot-prudue-set max ip level

	HA
		better set out of bound management ip address on another vlan or network in ha components

		higher priority is master

		conf sys ha
		set override enable
		end

		primary device after this must be primary but after trigger ha switch pair  at this moment one of the admins will be change slave priority to same master device
		if our las primary device come back to game  could not be master and here we have problem

		if set override on ha must set on pair or cluster of ha

		if forget set override role on each device could not negotiate together

		notice that if after switching pair (one has override another doesn't) secondary be master then set override with same priority level or number, our main master could not withdrawn

		after change  override setting on both device and set same priority, our secondary master able to retrieve the main roll cause from override and priority setup to manual switching the main device in cluster was secondary master this means after stable stage each modification will be retrieve us to stable state

		if disable override on wrong device ( secondary master ) and change priority to less value then enable it  could setup our main master and secondary master
		but set same priority on devices and do again disable and enable overrides our devices has no changed on main or second master
			in this state we use
				monitor link
				up time
				priority
				serial number
				*in pnetlab we have bug on time and priority
				------------------
				monitor link
				priority (higher)
				up time
				serial number

				in ha mechanism must setup  main device
				connect slave devices to primary
				after full synchronizations you can switch the roles and devices on cluster before synchronizations you have trouble on switch cause fgt-2 is empty and fgt-1 is full configured so here if switching happened our setting will be synchronized with fgt-2

		for memory utilization if exceed from threshold we have to switch on another device
			config system ha
			set memory-based-failover enable
			#default disable

			set memory-failover-threshold
			set memory-failover-monitor-period
			#intervals between threshold checking

			set memory-failover-sample-rate
			#between each monitor period how many time sample the memory usage

			set memory-failover-flip-timeout
			#all above conditions occures many times we can set a time to be relaxed then use it

			end

			*works on conserve mode means 0 percentage free in threshold part (full usage)
			in memory utilization concept we have a logic as master device if get fail on memory then switched to slave then master recover memory, this could not recover the rolls

		failover time in ha
			on heartbeat linkes we can use signaling times between 100 ms to 10ms
			default is 20ms

			config system ha
			set group-id 2
			set hb-interval 2
			#how many times send signals

			set hb-interval-in-millisecond 10ms
			#each side has maximum time to send and response signals

			set hb-lost-threshold 2
			#after many losts trigger ha

			end

		reserved management interfaces provide direct management access to each cluster unit, and give each cluster unit a different identity on your network. this simplifies using external services, such as snmp, to monitor separate cluster units.

		reserved management interfaces are not assigned ha virtual mac addresses. they retain the permanent hardware address of the physical interface, unless you manually change it using the config system interface command.

		reserved management interfaces and their ip addresses should not be used for managing a cluster using fortimanager. to manage a fortigate ha cluster with fortimanager, use the ip address of one of the cluster unit interfaces.

		configuration changes to a reserved management interface are not synchronized to other cluster units. other configuration changes are automatically synchronized to all cluster units.

		we have no cluster log, if login on  devices just see its own logs

		management features will be used in ha reserved management interface
			remote logging, including syslog, fortianalyzer, and forticloud
			remote authentication and certificate verification
			communication with fortisandbox
			netflow and sflow
			snmp queries and traps

			has dependencies as ha-direct option on management in terface and ha global setting to use the above services
				config system ha
				set ha-direct enable
				set ha-mgmt-status enable
				config ha-mgmt-interfaces
				edit 1
				set interface
				set dst
				set gateway
				end

				*these setups are not synchronized on cluster

				when working on snmp must set ha-direct config on primary device, then on snmp setup must make changes
					config system snmp
					edit 1
					config hosts
					edit 1
					set ha-direct enable
					end

		how upgrade firmware on clusters
			must check upgrade path tool

			when backup the device and start process of upgrading, have 2 model and strategies
				stage 1 - uninetrrupted upgrade
					all  devices are in a-a and cluster has live traffic flow
					without downtime process
					our master and primary device starts upgrade process and while upgrading another device get primary and manage traffic  flow
					this procedure is transparent from users


				stage 2 - interupted upgrade
					used downtime
					faster than uninetrrupted upgrade

					config system ha
					set uninterruptible-upgrade disable
					end

		statefull sctp (stream control transmission protocol)
			used for multihoming and path traverse
			when you want switch over between many isp with no 3-way handshaking
			used cached on 4-way handshake and session tables between vdoms and links
			by default don't use this if enable them between vdoms and over heart beat links our sessions will be sync

			config system setting
			set sctp-session-without-init enable
			end

			*if have streaming company better used tis to enable access without uninetrrupt

			in sctp packets we have verification tag which help us to manage and sync packets between vdoms and devices also used checksums to manage security of connections

			old generation of sct was sigtran or signaling transport with weakness of prevent dos attacks

		iccp (inter-control centercommunication protocol) & session-pickup in a-p mode
			between clients and server transmit all data
			over osi could have scanning feature on failover state
			integrity between security components and communication elements on tls or .. in application layers
			encryption and  authentication will be provide inside the iccp

			also iccp known as  inter-chassis control protocol which used for mc-lag to forward traffic between devices

			iccp exchange the information between active and passive device on cluster
			this get translate on fortios as session-pickup and sync session table

			mix 2 menaing of iccp (inter-chassis and inter-control)

			continuously scanning on switchover in failover and ha mechanism

			config sys ha
			set session-pickup enable
			end

			*if advertise  vmac on cluster beahve like garp (forwarding database (fdbs))

			diagnose sys ha mac
			#show us all cluster devices vmacs and main mac address
			#format of vmacs are
				group-prefix : group id hex : vcluster integer + idx
				-------------
				8 characters of 12

				gid part 1 >  0 - 255  > group prefix 00:09:0f:09
				gid part 2 >  256 - 511  > group prefix e0:23:ff:fc
				gid part 3 >  512 - 767  > group prefix e0:23:ff:fd
				gid part 4 >  768 - 1023  > group prefix e0:23:ff:fe

				hexadecimal id
					0 : 0 % 256 = 0  > 00
					255 : 255 % 256 = 255 > ff
					256 : 256 % 256 = 0  > 00
					511 : 511 % 256 = 255  > ff
					512 : 512 % 256 = 0  > 00

				vcluster integer is 00 for vclust 1 and 20 for vclust 2
				*if vdom is disable, all interfaces are in vcluster 1 bydefault

				for an ha cluster with:
				group id: 0 (hex: 00)
				virtual cluster: 1 (so <vcluster_integer> = 0)
				interface index: 3 (for example, port4)
				the vmac address would be calculated as follows:
				vmac = 00 - 09 - 0f -09-00-(0+ 3) = 00 - 09 - 0f - 09 - 00 - 03

				vmac=00-09-0f-09    -00-    (0  +     3)   = 00-09-0f-09-00-03
					 -----------	---- 	---      ----
					 group prefix   gid 	vclust 	 idx

			firmware upgrade in fgsp
				we have 2 cluster  that connected to eachother over loadbalancers

				in config sync (synchronizations without ha mechanism) we have no asymmetric routing and virtual cluster features

				FortiOS 7.0.2 added support for widening the HA virtual MAC address range. This change updated the session synchronization packet header structure

				fortigates running 7.0.2 or later, and fortigates running 7.0.1 or earlier will not accept session synchronization packets from each other
				if the traffic uses a new feature only available in a newer fortios version, it may not work when synchronized to an older fortios version

				pfcp (packet forwarding control protocol) support was added in 7.0.1, and a pfcp profile name was added to the sessions. when the sessions are synchronized to an older firmware version, the pfcp profile name will be lost and the sessions will not be able to handle the traffic as they would in 7.0.1

		nturbo is feature like offloading some process on asics , also called fast path
			config ips global
			set np-accel-mode basic
			#default is basic and enable

			*create direct data path from ingress port to egress port with ips features , has no more steps

			set cp-accel-mode advance
			#more type patterns matching, advance mode worked on devices with 2 or more cp8 or cp9

			*if has no command like this means you have no ipsa in device
			*default mode is basic

			there are some special cases (listed below) where sessions may not be offloaded by nturbo, even when nturbo is explicitly enabled in these cases the sessions are handled by the fortigate cpu :
				np acceleration is disabled
					auto-asic-offload is disabled in the firewall policy configuration

				the firewall policy includes proxy-based security profiles
					the sessions require fortios session-helpers
					ftp sessions are not offloaded to np processors because ftp sessions use the ftp session helper

				interface policies or dos policies have been added to the ingress or egress interface

				tunneling is enabled
					any traffic to or from a tunneled interface (ipsec, ipinip, ssl vpn, gre, capwap...) cannot be offloaded by nturbo


		*in some configurations, when a fortigate with np7 or np6 processors is operating with virtual clustering enabled, traffic cannot be offloaded by the np7 or np6 processors if the mac address of the vlan interface accepting the traffic is different from the mac address of the physical interface that the vlan interface has been added to
		if you are running a configuration like this, traffic from the vlan interface can be dropped by the np7 or np6 processors if you notice traffic being dropped, you can disable np offloading in the firewall policy that accepts the traffic to resolve the issue

		np7 and np6 offloading can still work in some network configurations when a vlan and its physical interface have different mac addresses
		offloading can still work as long as other network devices learn the fortigate's mac addresses from arp
		as well, offloading can work if the reply traffic destination mac is the same as the mac of the underlying interface

		get hardware npu np6 port-list
		diagnose npu np6 port-list

		get hardware npu np6xlite port-list

		get hardware npu np6lite port-list
		diagnose npu np6lite port-list

		*np6, np6xlite, and np6lite offloading is supported when you configure netflow for interfaces connected to np6,np6xlite, or np6lite processors. offloading of other sessions is not affected by configuring netflow
		full netflow is supported through the information maintained in the firewall session
		configuring sflow on any interface disables all np6, np6xlite, or np6lite offloading for all traffic on that interface

				speedtest

			on fortigate we can use fec on interfaces with 10g,40gm25g,100g
				config system interface
				ed sfp10
				set speed 40000full
				set media-type cr4
				set forward-error-correcrion cl91-rs-fec
				end

				*fec disable on 1000m 10g 40g also not supported
				*25g and 100g used automatically cl91-rs-fec

				sr: shortens the code by reducing the number of information bits
				lr: expands the code by lifting the parity-check matrix
				cr: represents the efficiency of the code (ratio of information bits to total bits)

				different types of optical transceivers or modules used for transmitting data over fiber optic cables
				these modules are typically used in high-speed data communication applications, such as in data centers or enterprise networks

				sr4 (short range 4):
					sr4 stands for short range 4-1ane
					it is designed for short-range multimode fiber (mmf) connections, typically up to 100 meters
					it uses four parallel fibers for transmission and four parallel fibers for reception, making it suitable for high-density environments like data centers
					commonly used in 40g and 100g ethernet applications

				lr4 (long range 4):
					lr4 stands for long range 4-1ane
					it is designed for long-range single-mode fiber (smf) connections, typically up to 10 kilometers or more
					like sr4, it uses four lanes for transmission and four lanes for reception, but it is optimized for longer distances
					commonly used in 40g and 100g ethernet applications where longer reach is required

				cr4 (copper 4) :
					cr4 stands for copper 4-1ane
					it is designed for short-range connections over copper cables, typically up to 5 meters
					it uses four twinaxial copper cables for transmission and reception
					commonly used in 40g ethernet applications within racks or between adjacent racks in data centers

					overview of .1q and .1ad
						the ieee 802.1q and ieee 802.1ad standards are both related to vlan (virtual local area network) tagging in ethernet frames, but they serve different purposes and functionalities

						ieee 802.1q
							single vlan tagging: the 802.1q standard allows for a single vlan tag to be inserted into an ethernet frame
							his tag includes a 12-bit vlan id, which can represent up to 4096 unique vlans (with a practical limit of 4094 due to reserved values)

							ethertype: the ethertype for a standard 802.1q tag is set to 0x8100, which identifies the frame as containing a vlan tag

							use case: it is widely used for basic vlan configurations, allowing network segmentation within a single broadcast domain

						ieee 802.1ad (qinq)
							double vlan tagging: the 802.1ad standard, also known as qinq (or "vlan stacking"), extends the capabilities of 802.1q by
							allowing multiple vlan tags to be nested within a single ethernet frame
							this effectively creates a 'vlan within a vlan scenario, enabling greater network segmentation and scalability

							ethertype: in contrast to 802.1q, the outer tag in an 802.1ad frame uses an ethertype of 0x88a8 for the service tag (s-tag), while the inner tag retains the standard ethertype of 0x8100 for the customer tag (c-tag)

							enhanced functionality: the addition of a second tag allows for more sophisticated operations, such as easier modification of frames by switches and better encapsulation of customer traffic by service providers
							the outer tag is primarily used to identify and segregate traffic from different customers

					features			ieee 802.1q 					ieee 802.1ad (qinq)
					tagging type 		single vlan tag 				double vlan tag
					ethertype 			0x8100 							outer: 0x88a8; inner: 0x8100
					vlan id capacity	up to 4096 unique vlans 		supports nesting for extensive segmentation
					use case 			basic vlan configurations 		service provider networks, metro ethernet
					additional features	none 							drop eligibility indicator (dei) replaces canonical format indicator (cfi) 24

				parallel redundancy protocol (prp)
					is a network redundancy protocol standardized by iec 62439-3:2016, designed for ethernet networks requiring high availability and short switchover times
					it is commonly used in critical infrastructure applications such as substation automation and industrial control systems

					key features of prp:
						dual network architecture
							prp operates by having each node connected to two independent, parallel local area networks (lans), known as lan a and lan b

						redundant packet transmission
							source nodes send duplicate packets over both networks simultaneously the destination node accepts the first packet received and discards the duplicate

						fail-independent networks
							the two networks are designed to be fail-independent, ensuring that if one network fails communication continues uninterrupted through the other network without any recovery time

						application transparency
							prp operates at layer 2, making it transparent to higher-layer protocols and applications2 this means no modifications are needed for existing protocols or applications

						cost considerations
							while providing high reliability, prp requires duplicating all network elements involved in redundancy, which can increase costs compared to other protocols like hsr (high availability seamless redundancy)

						however, it allows non-redundant devices to coexist on the same lan without needing special adapters or configurations

					use cases:
						substation automation
						industrial control systems
						high-power inverters
						synchronized drives
						prp offers advantages over traditional redundancy protocols like rstp by providing zero-time recovery from failures but at a higher deployment cost due to duplicated infrastructure requirement

					config system setting
					set prp-trailer-action enable
					end

					config system npu
					set prp-port-in port15
					set prp-port-out port16
					end

					config system virtual-wire-pair
					edit vwp-1
					set memeber port15 port16
					end

				policy route

				ecmp (equal cost multipathing)
					we have to set same destination with priority and distances on routing table (as static route)

					methods
						source ip base > divided equally between interfaces as session started froom same ip address to same path (default selection)

						weighted base > number of sessions that are connected through the interface the weight that you assign to each interface is used to calculate the percentage of the total sessions allowed to connect through an interface, and the sessions are distributed to the interfaces accordingly

						usage base (spillover or threshold) > The interface is used until the traffic bandwidth exceeds the ingress and egress thresholds that you set for that interface Additional traffic is then sent through the next interface member

						src-dst-ip-base > traffic is divided equally between the interfaces sessions that start at the same source ip address and go to the same destination ip address use the same path

						volume or measured volume base > this mode is supported in sd-wan only the workload is distributed based on the number of packets that are going through the interface

					config system setting
					set v4-ecmp-mode usage-base
					set ecmp-max-path 4
					end

					config system sdwan
					set status enable
					set load-balanace-mode usage-base
					end

							end

							*better change distances and priority on  routers

						load sharing
							if one interface get fail our traffic without switching or fail detection forwarded continuously
							*must used same distances and priority as ecmp or used pbr

							traffic behaviour without a link monitor is as follows
								if the remote gateway is down but the primary wan interface of a fortigate is still up, the fortigate will continue to route traffic to the primary wan this results in traffic interruptions

								if the primary wan interface of a fortigate is down due to physical link issues, the fortigate will remove routes to it and the secondary wan routes will become active traffic will failover to the secondary wan

						bgp next hop tag-match mode
							if need negotiate with isps on bgp tags, help you to manage them flexible

							config router bgp
							set tag-resolve-mode disable
								disable means resolve nexhop best match
								preferred means resolve nexthops with same tags, if not exist behave like best match
								merge means merge the best tags if they are different, shortcuts hide  parents, results will be exclude the nexthop tag match whose appeared on best match

								*merge used in loadbalancing mode
								*preferred used in failover mode

				route flap
					*resource intensive

					hold time
						config router bgp

						end

					dampening
						execute router clear bgp dampening 1.2.3.4/32
						execute router clear bgp flap-stat 1.2.3.4/32

						config router bgp
						set dampening enable
						set dampening-max-suppress-time 60
						#minutes_integer , after 60 mins if not reachable tell this link get really fail

						set dampening-reachability-half-life 15
						#minutes_integer , time of stable reachablity to this link after last failover

						set dampening-reuse 750
						#if were bigger than this number must wait to get relax and sure our link is valid

						set dampening-route-map rmp-fail-damp
						set dampening-suppress 2000
						set dampening-unreachability-half-life 15
						#minutes_integer

						end

						penalty: a value assigned to a route each time it flaps
						suppress threshold: if the penalty exceeds this value, the route is suppressed
						reuse threshold: if the penalty falls below this value, the route is reused (advertised again)
						half-life: the time it takes for the penalty to decay by half
						max suppress time: the maximum time a route can be suppressed

						a route flaps 3 times:
							penalty = 1000 x 3 = 3000
							since 3000 > 2000, the route is suppressed

							after 15 minutes:
							penalty decays by half: 3000 / 2 = 1500
							since 1500 > 750, the route remains suppressed

							after 30 minutes:
							penalty decays by half again: 1500 / 2 = 750
							since 750 ≤ 750, the route is reused (advertised again)

							*If the penalty doesn't decay below 750 within 60 minutes, the route will still be reused after 60 due to the max suppress time

					graceful restart
						config router bgp
						set graceful-restart enable
						set graceful-restart-timer 120
						#time of remote restart procedure

						set graceful-stalepath-time 180
						#time of recieved routes from ha pair or another remote device

						set graceful-update-delay
						#time of remote device (ha pair) main routing engine to manage the forwarding and routing

						config neighbor
						edit 1.2.3.4
						set capability-graceful-restart enable

	**************************************
	IPSec
		concept
			cia
				confidentiality
					if x1 andx2 are talking just these 2 x could realize , no more no less
					ensuring that information is accessible only to those authorized to have access

					protection from unauthorized access and use
					protecting data on systems, in transit,in process

				integrity
					if from x1 forward packet to x2  just see same packet not modified packets
					Safeguarding the accuracy  and completeness of information and processing methods

					Includes the detection of alterations that occurred in storage, transit, process, ...

				availablity
					if forward on time 1 recieved on time 2 not more 
					Ensuring that authorized users have access to information and associated assets when required

					acceptable level of performance
					fauit clerance
					prevention of data loss and
					destruction
					reliable backups, redundancy, ...

				these 3 components must be cosnidered as base trust and secure connection

			when need ipsec must used  mixed of many protocols
				encryption layer
					des (data encryption standard)
					3des
					aes (advance encryption standard)

					**here we can set encryption layer but hackers could used decryption methods and crack our secure layers so need another layer like authentication

				authentication layer
					md5 (message digest)
					sha (secure hash algorithem)
						160
						128
						256
						512

					**these called as integrity**

					after encryption we need a more secure layers like authentication to set valid and trusted users as reader
					here we have some methods
						presharedkey (just unique values between 2 side)
						rsa (a private and public key between 2 side)

						**base on method in authentication and integrity level (sha..) we mix data with this layers
							from source to destination we encrypt like this and how  decrypt on reciever?
								after decode by encryption method (preshared or rsa) and reverse integrity as sha160bit , we see simple data

								to be sure about trusted data and non-manipulated data we do encryption and integrity again

								*if were same value means trusted packet

				deffie-hellman
					dh-1 (760 bit)
					dh-2 (1024 bit)
					dh-5 (1500 bit)
					...

					on network we have key which has some key teeth must be same on both side

					**package all above compnents inside one section

				ipsec protocol
					ah (authentication header)(just protect headers not more)
						transport mode
							ip (global)
							ah header
							tcp
							data

						tunnel mode
							new ip header (external)
							ah header
							ip (internal)
							tcp
							data

					esp (encapsulation security payload)(protect whole data, port , header..)
						transport mode (main ip header and no new ip header)
							ip (global ip)
							esp header
							tcp*
							data*
							esp trailer*
							esp auth

							*protect part
								esp encrypt
									esp trailer + data

								esp authenticated
									esp encrypt + esp header

								**used protocol 50 

						tunnel mode (change ip header to fake ip header and hide main ip header(main destination) inside it)
							new ip header (external)
							esp header
							ip* (internal)
							tcp*
							data*
							esp trailer*
							esp auth

							*protect part
								esp encrypt
									esp trailer + data + internal ip header

								esp authenticated
									esp encrypt + esp header

								**used protocol 50 

					esp+ah

					ike (internet key exchnage)
						v1
							phase1 (tunnel creation or  control plane) (isakmp (internet security association and key management protocol) tuunel)
								used single proposal for each connection

								contain
									ike policy negotiation
										des , 3des ..
										integrity

									deffie-hellman groups
									authentication

								modes
									main
										initiator
											mm1 (authentication)
												sa (security association)
												vid

											mm3 (anti-reply)
												nonce (used for sequence for ipsec as anti-reply)
												ke
												vid

											mm5 (encryption)
												id
												auth
												cert
												cert req

										reponder
											mm2 (deffie-hellman groups)
												sa
												vid
												
											mm4 (create isakmp tunel)
												nonce
												ke
												vid
												cert
												req

											mm6 (encryption)
												id
												auth
												cert

										unencrypted and unauthenticated
											negotiation crypto setting
												mm 1 & 2

											secret key exchange
												mm3 & 4

										encrypted but unauthenticated
											prove identity
												mm 5 & 6

										--------------------------
										initiator new state mm_wait_msg2
											initiator sends encr/hash/dh ike policy details to create initial contact

											initiator will wait at mm_wait_msg2 until it hears back from its peer

										receiver new state mm_wait_msg3
											if receiver has this peer in the crypto map, it looks to see if it has a matching ike policy and sends its agreed ike policy back
											usually if the receiver stays at this state it means it doesn't have a return route back to the peer

										initiator new state mm_wait_msg4
											initiator sends a hash of its psk
											initiator will stay at msg4 until it gets a psk back from its peer
											if the receiver is missing a tunnel group or psk the initiator will stay at mm_wait_msg4

										receiver new state mm_wait_msg5
											receiver does not yet check if psk hashes match
											if receiver has a tunnel-group and psk configured for this peer it will send the psk hash to the peer
											if psks don't match, receiver will stay at mm_wait_msg5

										initiator new state mm_wait_msg6 or mm_active
											if psk keys match, initiator becomes mm_active and lets receiver know of match
											if psk doesn't match, initiator stays at mm_wait_msg6

										receiver new state mm_active
											receiver checks if psks match, if so it becomes mm_active and lets mate know

										phase1 complete

									aggresive
										am1
											contain
												mm 135
												key exchange
												authentication

										am2
											mm 246
											behave like initiator

										am3
											hash values
											authentication

										initiator
											isakmp header, sa, key, nonce, idii
											isakmp header, hash 

										responder
											isakmp header, sa, key, nonce, idir, hash_r
											ike sa established

							phase2 (data forward or data plane)(same with ike-v2) (ipsec tunnel)
								contain 
									ipsec securty association exchange (sa exchange)
										all approved items at above , mixed here and create tunnel to forward 

								mode 
									quick
										qm1
											peer

										qm2
											approving sa 

										qm3
											single way or unidirectional tunnel creation 
											differences between this and isakmp 
												isakmp is 2 way but this is one way

							**session learning mechanism
								detection on  requests and keepalive feature

						v2 (faster and lighter than v1 (used 4 message))
							phase1 (tunnel creation or  control plane) (isakmp tuunel)
								used proposal repository for many connection
									initiator
										ike sa init request
											vid
											sa
											ke
											nounce

									responder	
										ike sa init response
											vid
											sa
											ke
											nounce

									**merge negotiate crypto setting and secret key exchnage (unencrypted and unauthenticated)

							phase2 (data forward or data plane) (same with ike-v1) (ipsec tunnel)
								prove identity
									initiator
										ike auth request
											id
											auth
											cert
											sa
											ts
											nat 
											spi

									reponder
										ike auth response
											id
											auth
											cert
											sa
											ts
											nat 
											spi

									**encrypted but unauthenticated

							**features
								eap
									extensible authenticate protocol
									help us to provide authenitication with certificate

								lower bandwidth

								nat-traversal

								keepalive timers (built-in)

							**simulate message
								mm 1 to 4 will be on first message
									exchange ikev2 sa initiator

								mm 5 and 6 will be on second message
									exchange ikev2 authentication	

								request and response will be third message

							**per conenction behavior
								child sa
									from one conenction we create many stable child conenction base on edsa signature
										elliptic curve digital signature 
						---------------------------------------------------------
						ipsec concept ccnp
							has 2 phase
								phase 1 > management traffics
									we must negotiate on this step
										isakmp 
											version 1 (ikev1) > tunnel get up with what status
												has problem 
													for each tunnel connection profile must set seperated values but in ikev2 we
												
												set group of values with proposal
										
											version 2 (ikev2) > data can transfer through the phase 1 (flex vpn use this) (ipsec)
												more secure and faster on gdoi (group domain of interpretion (is faster and flexible))
										
												version 1 send 6 packet 
												version 2 sends 4 packets
												
												assymetric authentication in verson 2

								phase 2
									in each version is same but firs phase in each version is different
								
								default type of tunnels on cisco is gre

								int tunnel 0
								keep alive 3 3 (3 time chck with first value, 3 seconds wait fith second parameter)

								for site to site vpn must use ipsec
								and for ra (remote access) vpn must use ssl-vpn

				at the first steps we have isakmp tunnel then ipsec tunnel , inside this we have security association (sa) then inside sa we have spi , insied spi we have main mode or aggresive mode

				on cisco
					crypto isakmp policy 10
					encryption aes
					hash sha256
					authentication pre-share
					group 14
					#create isakmp policy for phase1 negotiate


					crypto isakmp key vpnuser address 10.0.0.2
					#specify the pre-shared key and the remote peer address  
					
					crypto ipsec transform-set myset esp-aes esp-sha256-hmac
					#create the phase 2 policy for ipsec negotiation

					access-list 100 permit ip 192.168.101.0 0.0.0.255 192.168.102.0 0.0.0.255

					crypto map mymap 10 ipsec-isakmp
					set peer 10.0.0.2
					set transform-set myset
					match address 100

					interface GigabitEthernet0/1
					ip address 192.168.101.1 255.255.255.0

					interface GigabitEthernet0/0
					ip address 192.168.102.1 255.255.255.0
					crypto map  mymap
					------------------------------------------
					crypto keyring preshare
					pre-shared-keyi address 11.12.13.2 key secret
					
					crypto isakmp policy 1
					encr 3des
					authentication pre-share
					group 5
					crypto isakmp profile preshare
					keyring preshare
					match identity address 11.12.13.2 255.255.255.255
					crypto ipsec transform-set aes-sha1 esp-aes esp-sha-hmac
					mode tunnel
					
					crypto ipsec profile ipsec-preshare
					set transform-set aes-sha1
					set isakmp-profile preshare
					
					interface tunnel111
					ip address 192.168.111.1 255.255.255.0
					tunnel source 11.12.13.1
					tunnel mode ipsec ipv4
					tunnel destination 11.12.13.2
					tunnel protection ipsec profile ipsec-preshare
					
					ip route 192.168.102.0 255.255.255.0 tunnel111

		auto discovery vpn combined with sdwan and help us to manage the hub and spoke or spoke to spoke ... connectivity

		phase 1
			security association and profiles

			auto negotiation and negotiate timeout

			mode config
				access to add some special features like address and services to some clients
				useful for dialup users

			nat traversal
				force network devices to use udp 4500 and 500 on ipsec connections

				ike version 2
					isakmp messages
						1
							send src ip + src port 500 to dst ip and dst port 500

						2
							reply on same values src and dst ip and port 500

						3
							send payload with nat-d or traversal as ip + port 500 and hash values
							192.168.1.1:500 = hash ip and port

						4
							check requested ip and port
							192.168.2.2:500 = different hash ip and port
							means we have some devices on network they change our values

				modes
					enable
						better use this

					force
						create hash and detect strictly

			keep alive frequency
				worked with nat-d
				nat device between peers may remove some long idle connections
				here we can set timer to keep alive checking mechanism and help to save resources

			dead peer detection
				used r-u-there messages and hashes on notify packages that client is live or not

				modes
					on-idle
						if one connection between devices were idle we could delete all components
						after 10 mins delete them
						active mode checking

					on demand
						while we have no traffic on links delete them immediately
						dpd-ack worked on passive mode

				dpd-retrycount
				dpd-interval

			forward error correction
				correct  errors on trasmissions with duplicating data

			add route
				if some clients worked like dialup users
				these peers would have specific routes on rib
				add some background routes into the devices

			device creation
				dialup users after connecting to device use virtual interfaces
				on devices add ip , tunnel id , ... on shells and detect and track the connections

			auto discovery sender and reciever
				used for mesh or advpn on large scale network
				if need advertise some routes or hello discoveries on routing protocols over ipsec must use them

			exchange interface ip
				in hub and spokes scenarios sometimes need to see branches directly without hub interference

			aggregate memeber
				some ipsec interfaces could be aggregate and make redundancy

			*if used dialup mode for ipsec cpn
			*specific peer id used for aggresive mode on ipsec
			*local id used when we are restricted to use specific peer id and define each side together

			xauth
				use user groups or chap or pap servers to authenticate connections

				auto/chap/pap server used on dialup users
				client  mode used for remote gateway and static connections

				*just one user group on auto server

			*better worked on any peer ip

		network-id
			config vpn ipsec phase1-interface
			edit x
			set network-id 2
			end

			with this we have different segmentation on ipsec behavioral over same public interface

		passive mode
			our device will not be initiator and wait to some request and sure about connections , even if we enable rekey or auto-negotiate or traffic initiated behind the fortigate

			fgt-1
				config vpn ipsec  phase1-interface
				edit link-1
				set rekey enable
				set passive-mode enable
				set passive-tunnel-interface enable
				end

			fgt-2
				config vpn ipsec  phase1-interface
				edit link-1
				set rekey enable
				set passive-mode enable
				set passive-tunnel-interface enable
				end

			*with this config no side and no negotiation will be happened so be careful about these if enable on both side cause trouble

		dead peer detection
			used some prob messages to detect remote side is alive or not
			each 5 second
			worked like icmp
			if have  high traffic links must set optimized probing
			dpd in phase 1 is 15 seconds

			config vpn ipsec phase1-interface
			edit link-1
			set dpd-retryinterval 15
			set dpd-retrycount 3
			end

			better set on-idle dpd mode for dialup ,  on-demand used for site to site vpns

			dpd scalability
				dialup sever and vpn connections must used optimized on-idle timming
				save resources

				diagnose vpn ike gateway list
				#show hmac hashs anad proposal used , authentication

				*if we have no dpd-ack our ike will be stop sending prob or dpd on intervals , dpd-ack used to connection termination

		*ike version 2 doesn't need authentication on phase 1 , has no xauth , used eap and mobike

		repeated autheniticated in ike version 2
			help us to recontrol and double check clients

			config vpn ipsec phase1-interface
			edit link-1
			set reauth enable
			end

			in ipsec gui, if select ike version 2 on authentication section see phase1 get change and use key-lifetime means after this time you have to reauth mechnaism

		quick crash detection (qcd)
			used in ike version 2
			like dpd  mechanism we have some probing and aliveness procedure
			faster detection

			on ike version 1 used some extentions for qcd (r-u-there and r-u-there-ack)
			on ike version 2 we have built-in features and information messages (on-demand dpd , reduce unnecessary traffics)
				some invalid and unprotected ike message recieved by links must set ivalid_ike_spi and invalid_spi notification payload

				qcd tokens contain private qcd secret base on ike spi generating mechanism also usd during ike authentication exchange

				*working on ike version 1 must add fortinet qcd on both side also not support aggresive mode on version 1 , just worked main mode

			config system setting
			set ike-quick-crash-detect enable
			end

		ike version 1 fragment
			sometime on udp transmission over isp infrastructure we have trouble so must change the udp packet size
			we can change it on phase 1

			larger than 576 byte in ipv4 and 1280 in ipv6 must fragment
			our packets will be retransmitted

			config vpn ipsec phase1-interface
			edit link-1
			set fragmentation enable
			end

			*bydefault is enable

		ike version 2 fragment
			can tune the fragmentation-mtu

			config vpn ipsec phase1-interface
			edit link-1
			set ike-version 2
			set fragmentation enable
			set fragmentation-mtu 500
			end

		embryonic limit
			protect ipsec from dos and ddos
			set maximum limit ipsec and ike requests

			config system setting
			set embryonic-limit 50
			end

			*worked on mid and hi-end devices with limitation 1000

		xauth
			pap > fortigate negotiate with client side
			chap > fortigate negotiate with server side
			auto-server > best mode to mix above models

			*just used one usergroup on auto-server,  we can mix many user container on one server

		*mode config and dynamic routes used to advertise all services and routes to fabric if limit ranges on phase2 ipsec we could not perform well

		mesh selector network
			our auto-discovery and exchange interafec ip on ipsesc gui advance part help us to advertise spoke-to-spoke connectivities
			on phase 2 help to merge and create full mesh networks together

			config vpn ipsec phase1-interface

			#must enable auto-discovery and exchange interface ip

			end

						or in interface gui pannel we could not find ipisec interface

				by check allow this feature all advance components in ipsec pannel will be used also aggregation interface ipsec
				but disable this means just use add-route feature

			gre over ipsec with cisco
				cisco
					interface tunnel1
					ip address 10.10.10.2 255.255.255.0
					tunnel source 20.20.20.2
					tunnel destination 20.20.20.1

					#phase1
					crypto isakmp policy 10
					encr des
					hash md5
					authentication pre-share
					group 2

					crypto isakmp key 123456 address 16.16.16.1

					#phase2
					crypto ipsec transform-set greipsec_transform esp-des esp-md5-hmac
					mode transport

					crypto ipsec profile greipsec_profile
					set transform-set greipsec_transform

					#vti
					interface tunne10
					ip address 20.20.20.2 255.255.255.0
					tunnel source 16.16.16.2
					tunnel destination 16.16.16.1
					tunnel mode ipsec ipv4
					tunnel protection ipsec profile greipsec_profile

					ip route 192.168.101.0 255.255.255.0 tunnel1

				fgt
					custom ipsec
						name ipsec-cisco-16
						remote ip 16.16.16.2
						incoming isp port
						preshared 123456
						ike vesion 1 , aggresive mode , any peer id
						des md5 , dh group  2
						on phase2 all subnets , des md5 , auto-negotiate , no pfs , protocol 47

					interface ipsec ip
						ip 20.20.20.1
						remote 20.20.20.2/24
						ping

					config system gre
					edit gre-16
					set interface ipsec-cisco-16
					set remote-gw 20.20.20.2
					set local-gw 20.20.20.1
					end

					on gre interface set ip
						ip 10.10.10.1
						remote 10.10.10.2/24

					route 192.168.106.0/24 use gre , if not found 192.168.106.0/24 blackhole

					policies
						incoming
							ipsec-cisco-16 + lan + gre-16

							ipsec-cisco-16  + lan + gre-16

						src and dst all
						service all
						nat disable
						log all sessions

						end

					preshared 123456 , aggresive mode , ike version 1 , any peer id, dh group 5 , des md5
					on phase 2 used all subnets and des md5 , dh group 5 , auto-negotiate , no pfs

					*create another ipsec link above but named link-2

					then on ipsec pannel select ipsec aggregate option
						name aggregate-links-12
						algorithem
							weighted round robin
							l3
							#perpacket loadbalancing with sdwan, cause use link-1 by pc 1 stop traffic forwarding when link-1 get fail and don't switch to  another link

							l4
							redundant (better)
							round-robin

						define memebers

			this setup is not suitable and useful for companies
			aggregate ipsec interfaces worked on round-robin mode
				config system ipsec-aggregate
				edit aggregate-links-12
				set algorithem round-robin
				end

				this mode is best practice for fortios
				all ipsec links get down after setting this command , must set same ad on static default gatway
				after these links will be valid

				*unset monitor command from ipsec phase1-interface
					config vpn ipsec phase1-interface
					edit link-2
					unset monitor link-1
					end

				end
				#these setup must be enabled to process with npu and coprocessors if disable them means use main cpu

				automatic asic offloading
					in new generation of processors we have some counters on os to help and improve performance
					when get disable all counters will be stop

					diagnose vpn ipsec stat

				*suite-b is a set of aes encryption with icv in gcm mode fortios supports suite-b on new kernel platforms only ipsec traffic cannot offload to npu cp9 supports suite-b offloading, otherwise packets are encrypted and decrypted by software

				null encryption will be on npu , des and 3des , aes  will be offloaded

				diagnose vpn ike gateway list
				#proposal show  hmac level

				config vpn ipsec phase1-interface
				edit link-1
				set ip-fragment pos-encapsulation
				#fragment packets after ipsec generate packets, we can set pre-encap

				we can set diffrentiated service code point on ipsec packets, if disable diffserve used main packet attributes
					*must disable npu offloading

					config vpn ipsec phase1-interface
					edit link-1
					set npu-offload disable
					next
					config vpn ipsec phase2-interface
					edit link-1
					set diffserv enable
					set diffserv  00011
					end

	SDWAN
		with sdwan can merge many links and create overlay (ipsec or another vpns) on them
		check link quality as main feature to help system best perfomance
			active probe
				set some active servers to used probes and generate consistent probe traffic

			passive  probe
				monitor and check some measurements on normal traffic flow from clients to wan
				check the threshold and qualities

		if some isp side interfaces wanna used dhcp feture on retrieving ip , gw ...  better set ad 10 instead 5 , because on sdwan we need use same ad to make loadbalance and better performance basically on link status measurements

		must clear all referer values on sdwan contributer component

		sdwan rules
			on outgoing part we can set measurements and types
				on interface section if select link 1 then link 2 means force use link 1 at the first stage then use anothers
				if wanna use smarter mechanism better use zone were you defined

				*policies for manage sessions and outgoing path
					config system sdwan
					config service
					edit 1
					set mdoe load-balance
					set hash-mode
						round-robin
						source-ip-base
						source-dest-ip-base
						inbandwidth
						outbandwidth
						bibandwidth
					end

			*policies about how use link and thresholds
			config system sdwan
			set load-balance-mode
				source ip (cli command: source-ip-based)(recommended)
					select this option to balance traffic equally between the sb-wan members according to a hash algorithm based on the source ip addresses

				session (weight-based)
					select this option to balance traffic equally between the sd-wan members by the session numbers ratio among its members
					use weight 50 for each of the 2 members

				source-destination ip (source-dest-ip-based)
					select this option to balance traffic equally between the sd-wan members according to a hash algorithm based on the source and destination ip addresses

				volume (measured-volume-based)
					select this option to balance traffic equally between the sd-wan members according to the bandwidth ratio among its members
			end

		link monitoring and failover on sla
			sdwan sla
				protocol ping
				server 3.3.3.1 (use different ip like google or ... except isp edge ip)
				sla targt
					if delay or jitter or packet lost get increase switch to another link

				link status
					interval of link health checking
					before  disabling links must see how many failures
					after link retrieved how many times check health check

					update static routes

			*check sla and  route accessabilities to make tuned scenario , some services and domains ... are blocked by goverments or organizations so must used accurated ip address to create stable sdwan scenario

			diagnose sys sdwan health-ckeck
			diagnose sys sdwan zone
			diagnose sys sdwan route-tag-list
			diagnose sys sdwan neighbor

		performance sla
			link health monitor
				check link quality with send and receive (round trip time rtt) packets on sdwan links
				protocols
					tcp connect
						half-open (syn , syn-ack check latency on trip time between these 2 packet send recieve)(default)
							not useful on waf publish and resource management

						half-close (fin , fin-ack latency base on these 2 packet trip time)

						config system sdwan
						set status enable
						config health-check
						edit tcpconnect
						set protocol tcp-connect
						set port 443
						set quality-measured-method half-close
						set server 192.168.20.200
						end

					ftp
						passive (ftp health check initiates and establishes data connection)(default)
						port (ftp server initiates and establishes data connections)

						config system sdwan
						set status enable
						config health-check
						edit ftp1
						set protocol ftp
						set port 21
						set user root
						set password 123
						set ftp-mode passive
						set ftp-file test_ftp.txt
						set server 192.168.20.200
						end

					dns (not recommended)
						send a record requests and expected ip address in responses

						config system sdwan
						set status enable
						config health-check
						edit 4_dns1
						set protocol dns
						set dns-request-domain iran.ir
						set dns-match-ip 1.2.3.4
						end

					tcp echo
					udp echo
						config system sdwan
						set status enable
						config health-check
						edit tcp-udp-test
						set protocol udp-echo
						port 7
						set server 192.168.20.200
						end

					twamp (two way active measurement protocol)(check latency on 2 way)
					owamp (one way active measurement protocol)
					http
						http get with link

					ping

				*if set high values on these components might have trouble on dos or real traffic detection better manage dos policies and these checking attributes

				*if set sdwan rule on manual or best quality strategies , sla target is an optional feature but change strategies to low cost sla or max bandwidth means need exact sla target to provide best performance
					latency threshold means last 30 probes calculation or jitter
					for packet lost check last 100 probes

					config system sdwan
					config health-ckeck
					edit 1
					set update-cascade-interface enable
					#if sdwan interfaces worked on group entity and inherite all components with enable status , disable make them single and ungroup then must change and set states with manual control

					set sla-fail-log-period 60
					set sla-pass-log-period 60
					#each 60 seconds  monitor sla fail or pass stats and events then log them

				probe modes in sdwan sla
					active
						send probes to determine link quality
						packets routed to internet and check links quality on main and slept links which contributed to sdwan
						both links used active probes on procedure

					passive
						use traffic to determine link quality. enable passive health check in policies to allow measurement
						till have traffic forwarding i have no checking , when one of the pairs or contributers get lost or fail i'v started checking and probing like active on another links to determine and detect link quality
						before link failing this mode send a minimum probes

					prefer passive
						same as passive mode, but send probes when there is no new traffic
						one link is active traffic forwarder and another is passive or slept
						on main link we have active mode checking and on another slept interface i have passive mode probing or link checking without side effect on main link

						link-1 (active) (sla-pass-pref)
						link-2 (passive) (sla-pass-pref)
							sla-pass-pref
								on link-1 check the active mode probing
								on link-2 check the passive mode probing if link-1 get fail , aware about link state and probes

					*when use passive modes policies must follow pasive-wan-health-measurement is enable auto-asic-offload will be disable

					what is the mos
						mos (meaning opining score) is a numerical measure of voice quality, ranging from 1 to 5:
							5: excellent quality (no distortion or noise)
							4: good quality (slight distortion, but call is clear) I
							3: fair quality (some distortion, call is understandable)
							2: poor quality (significant distortion, call is difficult to understand)
							1: bad quality (call is unusable)

						g.711: high-quality codec with no compression (mos ~ 4.2)
						g.729: compressed codec with good quality (mos ~ 3.9)
						g.722: high-definition codec (mos ~ 4.5)

						config system sdwan
						config health-check
						edit 1
						set mos-codec g711
						config sla
						edit 1
						set mos-threshold 3.6
						#higher means we need more reliabie and stable link to forwarding traffic

						end

						*for voip and sensetive data forwarding better use mode sla in sdwan services , mos could not working on priority or another modes

			default and gateway option
				config system sdwan
				config service
				edit 1
				set default enable
				#on this rule use fall-back option , on zone detect some failures in link-1 then check link-2 if have reachablity forward traffic then worked normally

				#on zone interfaces we have one link with more suitable states and use them for gateway

				end

				*all commands behave like above scenario , if disable all static routing .... but enable these we can forward traffic

				*by default they are disable and need tune fib and rib
					these two commands help adjust fortigate route selection by affecting how the fortigate consults the forward information base (fib)

					in order to decide whether an sd-wan policy-route can be matched, fortigate performs the following fib lookups
						fib best match for the destmation must return an sd-wan member
						fib route to the destination must exist over the desired sd-wan member

					when set default enable is used with set gateway enable, fortigate bypasses the fib checks, and instead routes any matching traffic of the sd-wan rule to the chosen sd-wan member using the member's configured gateway. sd-wan members must have a gateway configured

					when set default disable is used with set gateway enable, fortigate keeps the first rule in effect but causes the second rule to change to
						fib route to the gateway ip address must exist over any interface

			in an sd-wanconfiguration, the default route usually points to the sd-wan interface, so each active member's gateway is added to the routing table's default route. fortios uses equal-cost multipath (ecmp) to balance traffic between the interfaces
				source ip (source-ip-based)
					traffic is divided equally between the interfaces, including the sd-wan interface sessions that start at the same source ip address use the same path this is the default selection

				sessions (weight-based)
					the workload is distributing based on the number of sessions that are connected through the interface the weight that you assign to each interface is used to calculate the percentage of the total sessions that are allowed to connect through an interface, and the sessions are distributed to the interfaces accordingly sessions with the same source and destination ip addresses (src-ip and dst- ip) are forwarded to the same path, but are still considered in later session ratio calculations an interface's weight value cannot be zero

				spillover (usage-based) (better condition for sla)(flapping feature support on bandwidth)
					the interface is used until the traffic bandwidth exceeds the ingress and egress thresholds that you set for that interface additional traffic is then sent through the next sd-wan interface member

				source-destination ip (source-dest-ip-based)
					traffic is divided equally between the interfaces sessions that start at the same source ip address and go to the same destination ip address use the same path

				volume (measured-volume-based)
					the workload is distributing based on the number of packets that are going through the interface
					the volume weight that you assign to each interface is used to calculate the percentage of the total bandwidth that is allowed to go through an interface, and the bandwidth is distributed to the interfaces accordingly an interface's volume value cannot be zero

				config system sdwan
				set load-balance-mode source-ip-based
				end

		sdwan strategies
			in sdwan service or rule pannel we have some outgoing interface (strategies) methods like manual , best quality ...
			these selection method or strategies based on  sla and interface ranking

			config system sdwan
			config service
			edit 1
			set quality-link 3
				used some steps
					1 just used interface priority no sla

					2 used sla and routes without fall-back  feature if sdwan members get down all traffics will be drop , has no lookup mechanism or checking on rib

					3 used fall-back with sla also link monitoring

			in manual mode strategy we have no health-check feature or automatic selection just admin choose what happened , here behave like policy base route with application aware routing and bgp route tag supporting

				set hold-down-time 60
				end

				this comamnd means wait 60 seconds before switching back to primary interface event of a failover
				*in sdwan rule and outgoing interface strategies if set zones and interface preferred together they used first interface part then used zones, inside the interface part we have priority state if link-2 added first then link-1 means link-2 has higher priority than all another features

			we can use inband(downstream) and outband(upstream) or biband (bandwidth) throughput states with other attributes on sla targets

			maximize bandwidth
				config system sdwan
				set load-balance-mode source-ip-based
				#different with another hashing and multiplexing features

				config service
				edit 1
				set mode load-balance
				set hash-mode
					round-robin
						all traffic are distributed to selected interfaces in equal portions and circular order this is the default method, and the only option available when using the gui

					source-ip-base
						all traffic from a source ip is sent to the same interface

					source-dst-ip-based
						all traffic from a source ip to a destination ip is sent to the same interface

					inband
						all traffic are distributed to a selected interface with most available bandwidth for incoming traffic toward fortigate

					outband
						all traffic are distributed to a selected interface with most available bandwidth for outgoing traffic from fortigate

					biband
						all traffic are distributed to a selected interface with most available bandwidth for both incoming and outgoing traffic

					*advpn not support on this strategy , better set speed test feature and estimate bandwidth
					*if set strategy on maximum bandwidth , gateway commands will be unreachable (could use policy route)

				get router infor routing-table static

					*lowest cost (sla) even though all of the egress ports on specific routes (longest matched routes) are out of sla, the sd-wan rule still selects the first configured or lower-cost port from the egress ports to forward traffic

					*best quality (priority) even though the egress ports on specific routes (longest matched routes) have worse quality that all other ports on less specific routes, the sd-wan rule still selects the best quality port from the ports on specific routes to forward traffic

			overlay (sdwan with advpn)
				advpn (auto discovery vpn) is an ipsec technology that allows a traditional hub-and-spoke vpn's spokes to establish dynamic, on-demand, direct tunnels between each other to avoid routing through the topology's hub device

				the primary advantage is that it provides full meshing capabilities to a standard hub-and-spoke topology

				this greatly reduces the provisioning effort for full spoke-to-spoke low delay reachability, and addresses the scalability issues associated with very large fully meshed vpn networks if a customer's head office and branch offices all have two or more internet connections, they can build a dual-hub
				advpn network

				combined with sd-wan technology, the customer can load-balance traffic to other offices on multiple dynamic tunnels, control specific traffic using specific connections, or choose better performance connections dynamically sd-wan load-balance mode rules (or services) do not support advpn members

				other modes' rules, such as sla and priority, support advpn members

				on these type scenarios
					hub
						disable add route and devic creation
						enable auto-discovery sender

					spokes
						enable auto-discovery reciever and device creation
						disable add-route

					diagnose vpn ike gateway list
					#lists ike gateways
					#gateway name, ips, ike version, status

					diagnose vpn ike sa list
					#lists active ike sas
					#sa id, ips, algorithms, lifetime

					diagnose vpn tunnel list
					#lists all ipsec tunnels
					#tunnel name, ips, algorithms, status, traffic stats

					diagnose vpn ipsec sa list
					#lists active ipsec sas
					#sa id, ips, algorithms, lifetime, tunnel interface

					diagnose vpn ipsec restart
					#restarts ipsec tunnels
					#clears and re-establishes ipsec sas

					diagnose vpn ike restart
					#restarts ike negotiations
					#clears and re-establishes ike sas

					diagnose vpn ipsec packet
					#captures ipsec packet details
					#packet headers, encryption/decryption status


					which commands to use in which state/phase
						1. negotiating phase 1
							commands to use:
								diagnose vpn ike gateway list
								diagnose vpn ike sa list

							purpose:
								verify if the ike gateway is configured correctly.
								check if the ike sa is being established.

							what to look for:
								ensure the local and remote ip addresses match.
								verify the ike version (v1 or v2) and encryption/authentication settings.

						2. phase 1 up (ike sa established)
							commands to use:
								diagnose vpn ike sa list
								diagnose vpn ike sa filter

							purpose:
								verify that the ike sa is active.
								check the encryption and authentication algorithms used.

							what to look for:
								ensure the ike sa lifetime is valid.
								confirm that the ike sa is in the "established" state.

						3. negotiating phase 2
							commands to use:
								diagnose vpn ipsec sa list
								diagnose vpn ipsec sa filter

							purpose:
								verify if the ipsec sa is being established.
								check the phase 2 proposal settings.

							what to look for:
								ensure the local and remote subnets match.
								verify the encryption and authentication algorithms for phase 2.

						4. phase 2 up (ipsec sa established)
							commands to use:
								diagnose vpn ipsec sa list
								diagnose vpn tunnel list
								diagnose vpn tunnel stats

							purpose :
								verify that the ipsec sa is active.
								check the traffic statistics for the tunnel.

							what to look for:
									ensure the ipsec sa lifetime is valid.
									confirm that traffic is being encrypted/decrypted.
									if phase 1 is ok and ohase 2 get trouble , the name of second phase and ipsec niterface will not visible on  ipsec sa list

						5. tunnel down
							commands to use:
								diagnose vpn ike gateway list
								diagnose vpn ike sa list
								diagnose debug application ike -1
								diagnose debug enable

							purpose :
								identify why the tunnel is down.
								check for errors in phase 1 or phase 2.

							what to look for:
								look for error messages in the debug output.
								verify that the pre-shared key, ip addresses, and subnets are correct.

					summary of commands by state/phase
					state/phase commands to use purpose
						negotiating phase 1
							diagnose vpn ike gateway list
							diagnose vpn ike sa list

							verify ike gateway and sa establishment

						phase 1 up
							diagnose vpn ike sa list
							diagnose vpn ike sa filter

							verify ike sa is active and check algorithms.

						negotiating phase 2
							diagnose vpn ipsec sa list
							diagnose vpn ipsec sa filter

							verify ipsec sa establishment and check phase 2 settings.

						phase 2 up
							diagnose vpn ipsec sa list
							diagnose vpn tunnel list
							diagnose vpn tunnel stats

							verify ipsec sa is active and check traffic statistics.

						tunnel down
							diagnose vpn ike gateway list
							diagnose vpn ike sa list
							diagnose debug application ike -1

				hold down time

					end

				forward error correction (fec)
					Forward Error Correction (FEC) is used to control and correct errors in data transmission by sending redundant data across the VPNinanticipation of dropped packets occurring during transit
					The mechanism sends out x number of redundant packets for every y number of base packets

					ratio
						dynamically adjusted based on changes to the network sla metrics defined in the sd-wan sla health checks
						when there is no or low packet loss in the network, fec can work on a low redundant level sending only one redundant packet for every 10 base packets. as packet loss increases, the number of redundant packets sent can rise

						fec can be applied only to streams that are sensitive to packet loss
						policies that allow the udp based volp protocol can enable fec, while tcp based traffic policies do not
						his reduces unnecessary bandwidth consumption by fec

						because fec does not support npu offloading, the ability to specify streams and policies that do not require fec allows those traffic to be offloaded
						this means that all traffic suffers a performance impact

						base on loss percent
							0-10 %
								no redundant packet

							10-30 %
								just one redundant packet

							over 30 %
								per packet send duplicate or redundant packet

						config vpn ipsec phase1-interface
						edit link-1
						set npu-offload enable
						set fec-egress enable
						set fec-ingress enable
						set fec-health-check 1
						set fec-mapping-profile map-1
						set fec-base 10
						set fec-redundant 1
						end
						#enable these on 2 side
						#values are used when the link quality has not exceeded the limits specified in the FEC profile mapping , If fec-codec is set to xor the base and redundant packet values will not be updated

						config firewall policy
						edit 1
						set srcintf port3
						set dstintf sdwan-1
						set action accept
						set srcaddr 192.168.101.0/24
						set dstaddr all
						set schedule always
						set service ALL_UDP
						set fec enable
						next
						edit 2
						set srcintf any
						set dstintf any
						set action accept
						set srcaddr all
						set dstaddr all
						set schedule always
						set service ALL
						end

						configure fec mapping to bind network sla metrics and fec base and redundant packets
							config vpn ipsec fec
							edit map-1
							config mappings
							edit 1
							set base 8
							set redundant 2
							set packet-loss-threshold 10
							next
							edit 2
							set base 9
							set redundant 3
							set bandwidth-up-threshold 950000
							end

								the mappings are matched from top to bottom, packet loss greater than 10% with eight base and two redundant packets, and then uploading bandwidth greater than 950 mbps with nine base and three redundant packets

						#npu info: flag=0x82/0x81, offload=8/8, ips_offload=0/0, epid=249/74, ipid=74/86, vlan=0x0000/0x0000
							82 and 81 means offloading in system
							on tcp packets we have these on udp we have non offloading npu

						*non-fec protected tcptraffic is offloaded, while fec protected udp traffic is not offloaded

						diagnose sys sdwan packet-history
