				test the speed test on fortios
					manually run uploading speed test on the physical interfaces of each tunnel of an dial-up ipsec interface
						execute  speed-test-dynamic hs-ad-1 all n

					Manually run a non-blocking uploading speed test
						diagnose  netlink interface speed-test hs-ad-23

				diagnose debug application speedtest 100
				diagnose debug application speedtestd 100
				diagnose test application forticron 9
				diagnose test application forticron 10
				diagnose test application forticron 11
				diagnose test application forticron 12
				diagnose test application forticron 99
				#forticron used for schedules on fortios

				diagnose debug enable

				**a recurring speed test is configured that runs on the hub over the dial-up interfaces
				the speed tests are performed over the underlay interface from the hub to the spoke
				each spoke is configured to operate as a speed test server and to allow the speed test to run on its underlay interface
				the spokes establish bgp peering with the hub over the vpn interface and advertises its loopback network to the hub
				the specific configuration is only shown for fgt-spokes

				when the speed test is running, routing through the vpn overlay can be bypassed, and route maps are used to filter the routes that are advertised to peers. the spoke's route map does not advertise any routes to the peer, forcing the hub to use others paths to reach the spoke's network
				when no speed tests are running, the spoke's route map allows its network to be advertised on the hub

				when the speed test is complete, the measured egress bandwidth is dynamically applied to the vpn tunnel on the hub,
				and the result is cached for future use, in case the tunnel is disconnected and reconnected again

		deduplication packets in sdwan
			make a copy from generated packets
			prevent too much loads on asic and main processors

			sdwan zone deuplication means you have loadbalancing and redundant packets
			in rules means use the ha feature

			config system sdwan
			config duplication
			edit 1
			set srcaddr all
			set dstaddr all
			set srcintf port1
			set dstintf sdwan-1
			set service ALL
			set packet-duplication force
				disable
					disable packet duplication (default)

				force
					duplicate packets across all interface members of the sd-wan zone
					always active sacaning and check best forwarding to prevent duplication
					if see duplicated packets just forward one of them

				on-demand
					duplicate packets across all interface members of the sd-wan zone based on the link quality
					senese switch over between links and check duplication on packet (goes to scanning mode)

			set packet-de-duplication enable
			#default is disable , discarding of packets that have been duplicated

			set duplication-max-num 2
			#maximum sdwan zone members which could forward duplicate packets we can use 4 if have 4 links

			end

			*better use hub-and-spoke scenarios  , cause forward traffic will be on 2 advpn tunnle and this feature (duplication detection ) help us to prevent send copy or double packets to destination , after forwarding traffic over hub all spokes negotiation will be directly

			or

			config sys sdwan
			config service
			edit 1
			...
			end
			config duplication
			set service-id 1
			set packet-duplication force
			end

			packets can be on sdwan rule and
				forced to duplicate to all members of the same sd-wan zone , in spoke 1 set packet-duplication to force so that when a client sends a packet to the server, it is duplicated to all members of the same zone as long as its health check is alive if a members health check is dead, then the member is removed from the sd-wan duplication zone

				duplicated to other members of the sd-wan zone on-demand only when the condition of the link is  not good enough set packet-duplication to on-demand if sla-match-service is disabled, when all the slas of the member exceed threshold (sla_map=0), the packet is duplicated but when the slas are within threshold (sla map != 0), the packet is not duplicated if sla-match-service is enabled, then only the sla health checks and targets used in the service rule need to exceed threshold in order to trigger packet duplication

				packets can be duplicated to all members of the same sd-wan zone when the traffic matches one or more regular sd-wan service rules

			ha-cluster and sdwan
				some devices with hardware switch could help us save money
				on some scenarios with redundancy or ha monitoring feature we need extra resources like cable links or radios
				with hardware switch feature on some fortigates we can forward traffic between ha heartbeat links and active monitor links on each fortigates

				isp-1 			isp-2
				|	*			/	|
				| 	 ************	|
				|	////////////*	|
				|  /			*	|
				fgt-1 			fgt-2
					------------
					ha-heartbeat
					------------

				in this sd-wan configuration, two fortigates in an active-passive (a-p) ha pair are used to provide hardware redundancy instead of using external switches to provide a mesh network connection to the isp routers, the fortigates use their built-in hardware switches to connect to the isp routers

				only fortigate models that have hardware switches can be used for this solution
				ports in a software switch are not in a forwarding state when a fortigate is acting as a secondary device in a a-p cluster

				a link on a hardware switch cannot be monitored in ha monitor
				so it is impossible to perform link failure when a port in either of the hardware switches fails
				performing a link failure is unnecessary in this configuration though, because any link failure on the hardware switch will be experienced by both cluster members
				sd-wan sla health checks should be used to monitor the health of each isp

				*if set this scenario consider routes and rules must be redesigned

			hub sends a shortcut offer to a spoke every five seconds

				#default

				end

			mpls concept for fortinet nse-7
				mpls
					labels:
						think of labels like sticky notes attached to a packet. these labels tell the network devices (routers or switches) where to send the packet next labels are small (32 bits) and are added to the packet between the layer 2 (data link) and layer 3 (network) headers

					label switching:
						routers in an mpls network are called label switching routers (1srs)
						when a packet enters the mpls network, the first router (called the ingress router) adds a label to it
						each subsequent router swaps the label with a new one (based on a pre-determined path) and forwards the packet		the last router (called the egress router) removes the label and sends the packet out of the mpls network

					label switched path (1sp):
						this is the pre-determined path that packets follow through the mpls network think of it as a "tunnel" & "highway" for packets to travel from the source to the destination

					forwarding equivalence class (fec):
						packets with the same characteristics (e.g., same destination, same quality of service requirements) are grouped into an fec all packets in the same fec get the same label and follow the same path

				components of vpnv4
					route distinguisher (rd) :
						a unique identifier added to customer routes to make them distinct in the mpls network

						format: <asn:number> or <ip:number> (e.g., 65001:100 or 192.168.1.1:100)

						if a customer uses 10.1.1.0/24, the vpnv4 address becomes 65001:100:10.1.1.0/24

					route target (rt):
						a tag that controls which routes are imported or exported between vrfs (virtual routing and forwarding tables)
						used to define which customer sites can communicate with each other

						rt 65001:200 might be used to connect all sites of customer a

					vpnv4 address:
						a combination of the rd and the customer's ipv4 address

						65001:100:10.1.1.0/24

				traffic flow
					traffic originates at site a:
						device 10.1.1.1 sends traffic to 10.1.2.1

						traffic is sent to cel

					cel forwards traffic to pel:
						ce1 forwards the traffic to pe1

					pe1 encapsulates traffic:
						pe1 adds a vpn label (100) and a transport label (200)
						packet: [200][100] [10.1.1.1 = 10.1.2.1]

					traffic travels through the mpls core:
						p1 swaps the transport label (200 - 300)
						p2 swaps the transport label (300 - 400)

					traffic reaches pe2:
						pe2 removes the transport label (400) and uses the vpn label (100) to identify the vrf
						pe2 removes the vpn label and forwards the traffic to ce2

					ce2 delivers traffic:
						ce2 delivers the traffic to 10.1.2.1

					| transport label (200) | vpn label (100) | ip packet (10.1.1.1- 10.1.2.1) |

			segmentation requires that vrf info is encapsulated within the ipsec vpn tunnel
			this setting enables multi-vrf ipsec tunnels

				config vpn ipsec phase1-interface
				edit link-1
				set encapsulation vpn-id-ipip
				end

			role of a vrf can be specified, along with other vrf details
			up to 64 vrfs can be configured per vdom for any device

				config router bgp
				config vrf
				edit 20
				set role ce
					ce
						realize vpnv4 , vrf ,...

					pe

					standalone

				set rd <string>
				set export-rt <route_target>
				set import-rt <route_target>
				set import-route-map rmp-test
				config leak-target
				edit 10
				set route-map <route-map>
				set interface <interface>
				end

				or

				config router bgp
				config vrf
				edit 0
				set role pe
				next
				edit 10
				set role ce
				set rd 1:1
				set export-rt 1:1
				set import-rt 1:1
				next
				edit 20
				set role ce
				set rd 2:1
				set export-rt 2:1
				set import-rt 2:1
				next
				end

			vsa and avp
				vsa (vendor specific attributes) (used for specify hardware or vendor)
				avp (attribute value pair)(used for standard radius)

				also  merge type length values (tlv) to manage the components
					1 > username

					4 > nas ip address or nas (network access server) means with ip x you negotiate with radius server

					8 > framed ip address

					25 >  class accounting packets and requests for devices like ap , firewall , .... , behave like sso on all radius systems

					26 > fortinet vsa

					32 > nas identifier

					42 > acct-input-octed
					43 > acct-output-octed

					44 > acct-session-id

					55 > event-timestamp

					avp encapsulate the vsa inside the packets and requests
						detect the vsa automatically and put them on radius server

						vendor fortinet 12356
							begin-vendor fortinet

							attribute fortinet-client-ip-address 2 ipaddr
							attribute fortinet-vdom-name 3 string
							attribute fortinet-client-ipv6-address 4 octets
							attribute fortinet-interface-name 5 string
							attribute fortinet-access-profile 6 string

						for example code 1 used for group name in radius while you are using custome attribute number 26 and fortinet vsa 12356 as vendor id

		fortitoken
			has 6 or 8 digit number as otp to make more secure access for some clients or admins
			need special license
			the codes or tokens called seeds
			has 20 digit code on registeration

			execute fortitoken-mobile import 00000
			show user fortitoken
			diagnose fortitoken info
				new
					available
					newly added, not pending, not activated, not yet assigned


				active
					assigned to a user, hardware token

				provisioning
					pending
					assigned to a user andwaiting for activation on the fortitoken mobile app.

				provisioned
					assigned to user and activated on the fortitoken mobile app

				provision timeout
					token provided to user but not activated on the fortitoken mobile app. to fix, the token needs to be re-provisioned and activated in time

				token already activated, and seed won't be returned error
					token is locked by fortiguard fds. the hardware token wasalready activated on another device and locked by fds

				locked
					either manually locked by an administrator (set status lock), or locked automatically, for example, when the token is unassigned and the forticare ftm provisioning server was unreachable to process that change

				config system global
				set two-factor-ftm-expire 72
				#base on hour

				end

			Identifying users based on their client certificate
				When the client's certificate is valid, or mandatory-ca-verify is disabled, the FortiGate can then inspect the certificate to check specific fields for matching values
				There are three ways of specifying which certificate field to verify: by subject, CN, or LDAP. All string comparisons are case sensitive

				subject
					config vpn certificate setting
					set subject-match substring
					#contains distinguished name , each string on subject partition recieved could be consider as inspected value , *varzesh is a substring  of varzesh3.com.
					#value means you pointing to main value of recieved string mean if set varzesh just inspect the varzesh not varzesh3.com

					set subject-set superset
					#all components in subject or full distinguished name must be visible if set subset means some parts of this will be used

					set cn-match substring

					set cn-allow-multi enable
					#could use many cn

					end

				cn
					Commonname(CN)certificate verification compares the CN in the subject field with the configured string (such as set cn "jcarrey")

					The CN type also controls the format checking of the CN string
					In this example, if the CN type is set to  , the CN  must be in email format (set cn jcarrey@fortinet.com")

				ldap
					ldap-integrated user authentication allows the fortigate to check the connecting user against an ldap in two ways: through a username and password, or the certificate's principal name
					the password method requires the username and password of each authenticating user to be entered, so it is not recommended when configuring pki users

					the principal-name method is recommended

					the upn in the user certificate's subject alternative name field is used to look up the user in the ldap directory
					if a match is found, then authentication succeeds
					this type of configuration scales well since only one pki user needs to be created on the fortigate
					connecting clients use their unique user  to match within the configured ldap server

		fsso
			detects the logon event and records the workstation name, domain, and user,
			resolves the workstation name to an ip address,
			determines which user groups the user belongs to,
			sendstheuser logon information, including ip address and groups list, to the fortigate unit, and
			creates one or morelogentries on the fortigate unit for this logon event as appropriate

			security fabric  > external connectors
				fsso
					name fsso-ldap
					primary fsso agent 192.168.20.200

						here we have mode collector agent and local

							local means fortigate check users credential and push them to ad if was valid access user to resources

			user & authentication
				create group as fsso and select user groups on them
				name fsso-group

			dc agent and fsso used for sso but
				fsso means integrated users (ldap ,novel ....other users repositories) fortigate and ad with fortiweb ... another all fortigate devices also with many forest or domains

				dc agent means just worked on active director and limited domian and just fortigates

			on ad server
				must work with fsso 5.0.0.3.. if you are working on fortios 7.2.0 or higher
				better inistall fsso instead of dc agent

				then used advance mode and dcagent (could use another mode , on fortigate doesn't matter change collector agent to local if set on polling mode in ad could worked on collector agent in fsso fortigate)

			limitations
				ntlm based authentication is not supported

				if there are a large number of user logins at the same time
				the fsso daemon may miss some consider using fsso agent mode if this will be an issue

				the fsso daemon does not support all of the security log events that are supported by other fsso scenarios
				only kerberos log in events 4768 and 4769 are supported

		polling ad server
			security fabric > external connectors
				polling ad
					has no dc agent installation so just need define ldap server then negotiate with ad and fetch login credentials
					just need ssoadmin or admin level user

					if set user groups on policies when you need go to internet open login pages

		*differences between fsso local mode and polling mode ad
			polling mode just fetch credentials after login page and have no flexiablities if need restric access on local organization resources
			fsso local used automatic cerdential checking on logins and forward them to fortigate (collector agent forward from server to fortigate all logons information but local means fortigate forward or try to fetch logons information)

			*if have many servers for ad or users repositories better use dcagent and if have on e ad or user repository better use local mode fsso

			*if ad were busy and could not forward credential as dc-agent and fsso collector agent to fortigate we can use redundant mechanism as polling mode ad to fetch credentials by fortigate

			 		user="{ { :domain}} \\{ { : username}}"

			 		event id 			description relevant for fsso?
			 		4624				successful logon (interactive, network, remoteinteractive)
			 		4625 				failed logon (unless tracking failures)
			 		4634				logoff (for session termination)
			 		4647 				user-initiated logoff
			 		4776 				ntlm authentication (for non-interactive logons) (if using ntlm)
			 		4768				kerberos tgt request (for domain logons) (if using kerberos)

			 		<event><system><eventid>4624</eventid> ...< eventdata>
			 			<data name="targetusername">x</data>
			 			<data name="targetdomainname">hq</data>
			 			<data name="ipaddress">192.168.1.1</data>
			 			<data name="logontype">3</data>
			 			<data name="workstationname">pc2</data>
			 		</eventdata></event>

			 fsso collector agent (recommended for most enterprises)
			 	a separate windows-based agent (installed on a domain controller or member server) collects ad login events and
			 	forwards them to fortigate
			 	supports multiple ad domains and multiple fortigates

			 	advantages
			 		more reliable - uses polling mode (checks ad logs continuously)
			 		supports multiple dcs - can monitor multiple domain controllers
			 		works with ntlm & polling - can use windows event logs (ntlm) or direct ldap polling
			 		better for large networks - scales well in enterprise environments

			 	disadvantages
			 		requires a windows server (extra component to manage)
			 		slightly higher latency (~1-2 minutes to detect logoffs)

			 	when to use?
			 		enterprise networks with multiple dcs

			 	when high reliability is needed
			 		if ntlm authentication is restricted (use ldap polling instead)

			 local fsso mode (direct on fortigate)
			 	fortigate directly queries ad (no separate agent needed)
			 	uses dc agent mode (ldap polling) or ntlm authentication (windows event log monitoring)

			 	advantages
			 		no extra software (runs directly on fortigate)
			 		faster deployment (no need to install an agent)
			 		works in smaller environments (single dc setups)

			 	disadvantages
			 		less reliable (depends on ad connectivity)
			 		limited scalability (struggles with multiple dcs)
			 		no support for multiple domains (single ad only)

			 	when to use?
			 		small networks (single dc)

			 	temporary setups (quick deployment)
			 	if windows server installation is not possible

			 key differences summary
			 	feature 					fsso collector agent 				local fsso mode
			 	installation 				requires windows-based agent		no extra software
			 	scalability supports        multiple dcs & domains			 	single dc only
			 	detection method            polling (ldap) or ntlm			 	dc agent (ldap) or ntlm
			 	reliability            		high (independent of fortigate)		medium (depends on fortigate-ad connection)
			 	best for            		enterprises, large networks			small offices, quick setups

			 	which one should you choose?
			 		use fsso collector agent if:
			 			you have multiple dcs or complex ad environments
			 			you need high reliability (eg, for compliance)
			 			you want centralized logging & control

			 		use local fsso mode if:
			 			you have a single dc and simple setup
			 			you can't install an agent on a windows server
			 			you need a quick deployment

			ttl setup consideration
				security optimization
					shorten ttl for high-risk services (e.g., http to 300 seconds instead of default 3600
					financial transactions (set lower ttl for banking sessions)
					guest wifi networks (reduce ttl to force more frequent re-authentication)

				performance tuning
					long-lived connections (increase for backup/vpn sessions)
					voip/media streaming (match ttl to expected call duration)
					database connections (extend for persistent application connections)

				network problem resolution
					nat table exhaustion (reduce ttl when hitting session limits)
					dos mitigation (lower ttl for icmp/udp flood protection)
					stale session cleanup (aggressive timeouts for udp protocols)

				config system session-ttl
				get

			if reach ttl 0 means fortigate sent flag rst or fin to complete the session but on never state we have no flags

				----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
				configuration location 		directly in firewall policies 		in security profiles, applied to policies

				management 					more policies needed 				fewer policies with profile reuse
				recommended by fortinet		yes (current recommendation) 		legacy approach
				ideal for 					modern deployments, zero-trust 		simple networks, migration scenarios

			learning mode
				used policy analyzer mea (management extension application) to detetct required policies and ... also manage the policies

				application (mea) that is released and signed by fortinet to run on fortimanager

				policy analyzer mea is an automated tool with a wizard
				it works with security policies in learn mode from a managed fortigate to analyze logs sent to fortianalyzer based on the analyzed traffic, administrators can
					block malicious traffic
					allowed learned traffic - permissive mode (use all log analyzing to generate the policies)
					allowed learned traffic - restricted mode (use utm logs to generate policies)

				a policy block is automatically created and inserted in the policy package, and the policy package is installed
				to the target fortigate

				the following limitations apply when learn mode is enabled in a security policy
					only interfaces with device-identification enable can be used as source interfaces in a security policy with learning mode enabled
					incoming and outgoing interfaces do not support any
					internet service is not supported
					nat46 and nat64 are not supported
					users and groups are not supported
					some negate options are not supported

					*after set learning mode on policies we have no traffic access on clients , on firewall we can see logs and .. which used to access the resources and point the learn term in policy-mode section

			scanning type
				config ips global
				set anomaly-mode
					#if user sent 20 packets toward device and our limits seted on 10

					continuously
						at the first time recieved 10 packets
						then drop all another packets
						also close the connections

					periodical
						after recieved 10 packets did not close the connections just take a break and on another time interval access the packet forwardings

				end

					#id=icmp_flood ip=192.168.2.50 dos_id=1 exp=998 pps=46 freq=50
						in time sequence 0 to 1 second we have limits (pps 46 (packet per seconds) what means you have a acceptable rate
						freq 50 means on time sequence 0-1 you send 4 more packets
						exp means on another time sequences aggregate remained 4 packets and reach this values

				**the banned user list is kept in the kernel, and used by antivirus, data leak prevention (dlp), dos, and intrusion prevention system (ips)
				any policies that use any of these features will block traffic from the attacker's ip address

		access control list
			supprt by some devices
				100d, 100e, 100ef,101e
				140d, 140d-poe,140e,140e-poe
				1500d, 1500dt
				3000d, 3100d,3200d,3700d
				all 300e andlarger e-series models
				all 100f and larger f-series models

				on fortigate models with ports that are connected through an internal switch fabric with tcam capabilities
				acl processing is offloaded to the switch fabric and does not use cpu resources

				vlan interfaces that are based on physical switch fabric interfaces are also supported

				interfaces that are connected through an internal switch fabric usually have names prefixed with port or lan, such as port1 or lan2; other interfaces are not supported

				the packets will be processed by the cpu when offloading is disabled or not possible, such as when a port on a
				supported model does not connect to the internal fabric switch

				config firewall acl
				edit 1
				set interface port2
				set srcaddr all
				set dstaddr vip
				set service telnet
				next
				end
				config firewall ac16
				edit 1
				set interface port2
				set srcaddr all
				set dstaddr iis
				set service telnet
				end

			concept realize
				model series 		switch fabric / npu ports 			non-offloaded ports 	notes
				--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
				fortigate 60e/f 	port1-port7, internal4				wan1, wan2, dmz 		port8 (60f only) uses cpu
				--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
				fortigate 100f 		port1-port24 (np6 asic) 			wan1, wan2				ports 1-8 share the same 																				 npu bank
				--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
				fortigate 200e		port1-port16, ha1, ha2 (np6 asic)	wan1, wan2				ha ports can offload
				--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
				fortigate 600e		port1-port24, x1-x2 (np6 asic)		wan1, wan2				10g ports (x1/x2) offload
				--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
				fortigate 1800f		port1-port48 (np7 asic)				wan1, wan2, mgmt		np7 handles 100g traffic
				--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

				tcam (ternary content-addressable memory) enables fast acl/rule matching in hardware
					used on layer 2 or layer 3

				on layer 3 we use tcam and on layer 2 use cam

				cisco express forward (cef) before processing packets by cpu must visit the cef (on fortigate called np, cp , soc ..) cause need checking cache to forward packets faster

				fib tcam checks vpn , ip layer , mask and labels

				data plane or forwarder section contains cef (fib table (layer3) , adjancency table (arp table))
				and control plane contains cpu process arp table and ip routing table processing (if get drop or timeout about connections must check cef and data plane part)

				cef is like hardware feature or tcam and fib is software cef which contains rib and adjancency tables

				cef punted (cpu must check at first time)
				cef switch ( used asic and cached values)

			diagnose firewall acl counter
			diagnose firewall acl clearcounter

		interface policies
			before checking security policies we can provide prior checking or security layers
			worked on flow base
			worked on pre-forward path (before access npu or .. we can limit access)

			behave like on-arm or packets destined to fortigate , droped or flooded multicast traffic on fortigate

			dynamic source route inject (dsri)
				fortigate detect the policies with some interface on this deamon and change the path or routes
				*when use policy route or sdwan this feature will be helpful
				**ips blocking or security checkings  will be same as normal behave but after enabling this our ips and .. might be bypass

				**dynamic routes and adjancency tables will be effects on these policies

				**better check  this feature while you have asymmetric routing and got trouble on forwarding packets , if enabled our routing-table get confused

				config firewall interface-policy
				edit 1
				set status enable
				set logtraffic all
				set interface port3
				set srcaddr all
				set dstaddr all
				set service all
				set dsri disable
				set webfilter-profile-status enable
				set webfilter-profile wf-test

				set ips-sensor high
				end

				*must set normal policies to access another components like lan-wan then create interface policy to limit access on specific destination or targets

		source nat
			xlate / session / nat table

			per ip we have 0-65535 port (normally use 50000 random port)

			preserve source port  needed on some scenarios which applications track your sessions on requested connections

			policy & object
				ip pool
					overload or pat
						we have a ip on edge 192.168.254.252/24
						manage 0-65535 or 50000 port on connections
						worked like normal nat mechanism
						usually companies need this not more

						net.ipv4.ip_local port range
							has table with random port generated inside it
							assigned client requests or connections on these ports
							tcp connections 5 min timeout and udp 3 minutes

							consider we have 2 ip on edge
								each ip has 60416 port

							src ip is 192.168.101.1

							ip pool 192.168.254.2-192.168.254.3
								the block size of ip is 2 (we have 2 useful ip)

							which ip pool will forward requests
								192.x.x.x
									each x contains 8 bit , if set 1 on all reach 256
									192 * (256 . 256 . 256) or 192 *(256 pow 3) = 3,221,225,472
								+

								192.168.x.x
									192.168 * (256 pow 2) = 11,010,048
								+

								192.168.101.x
									192.168.101 * (256) = 25,856
								+

								192.168.101.1
									1

								=
									3,232,261,377

								3,232,261,377 mod 2 = 1

								this means used ip 1 in block range which you bought from isp
									192.168.254.252 is our outgoing ip address
									used 60416 port assignment to client requests from this ip

										3,232,261,377 mod 60416 = 5,377
											this is the scope of ports will be assigned to source ip 192.168.101.1

										5,377 + 1024 = 6,401
											from port 1024 to 6401 will be used by 192.168.101.1

					one to one
						some applications need just one dedicated ip address
						some servers need more port

						for each client ip  and port (private) we have mapped edge ip and port (public)

					fixed port range
						if need applications or connections on same translated ip address
						better used for sip or voip scenarios

						we have limitation on client side port per user assignment (we can set minimum 32 port)
						if set 0 means behave like pat
							for sip or voip need (rtt / sip) 50 - 100
							general nat 256
							enterprise 512

						also we have arp reply feature

						on kali
							do i in (1..60416); do curl -s 20.20.20.1 & done

					port block alocation
						useful on cgnat feature
						consider /29 ip block
						5 useful ip address on client side
						block size means 5 ip address must spilited to x size or segment
						how many port will be used by users  on each segment x

						block size 128
						block per user 8

						each ip spilited to 128 port and each user has 8 segments so 8*128 is maximum ports will be used by users

						ip block
							192.168.254.252-192.168.254.252

						block size 8
						block per user 128
							8*128 > 1024

						each ip has 60416 port

						60416 / 128 > 472 (maximum port used by each user)

						60416 / 1024 > 59 (this count of internal ip will be handle , another work 59 client with 1024 port could connect to fortigate  59*1024 > 60416)

		map-e support
			between  ipv4 and ipv6 network plane switching we used them

			carrier grade (cgnat)
			on isp and enterprise networks
			7000,4000,3000,2000or vm series

			mobile security
				gprs tunneling protocol (gtp) (used on 4g and 5g cell networking)
				packet forwarding control protocol (pfcp)

			mvno - mobile virtual network operators

			used part number fcr-eupg
				supported FortiGate models
					260xF
					3000 series and above
					VM08/VM08-v
					VM16/VM16-v
					VM32/VM32-v
					VMUL/VMUL-v
						**VM S-Series is not supported

			with vne (virtual network tunnel enabler and encapsulator) encapsulate ipv4 into the ipv6

			when need use cgnat?
				when we have single ip on edge
				each ip has 60416 port
				we can set some ports to many users

				some ce devices toward client side provide nat feature or mechanism

				between ce devices and cgnat device we have range 100.64.0.0

				client --- ce decives --- 100.64.0.0 --- cgnat device (or border relay devices) ---- r or isp router

				client send data as 192.168.1.1:50000 to ce device
				ce device make nat on client packet and translate them to 100.64.1.1:50000
				then cgnat device do this again and forward packets to isp router
				isp router with single ip and single port replies all requests

				map-e is nested nat mechanism

			dynamic policies
				fabric devices
					the list of firewall addresses includes a default address object called fabric_device. you can apply the fabric  , device object to the following types of policies
						firewall policy, including virtual wire pairs, nat 46, and nat 64 (ipv4 only)
						ipv4 shaping policy
						ipv4 acl policy

					you cannot apply the fabric_device object to the following types of policies
						ipv4 explicit proxy policy

					you also cannot use the fabric device object with the following settings
						custom extension on internet-service
						exclusion of addrgrp

					the fabric device object address values are populated based on
						fortianalyzer ip (from the fabric settings pane)
						fortimanager ip (from the fabric settings pane)
						fortimail ip (from the fabric settings pane)
						forticlient ems ip (from the fabric settings pane)
						fortiap ips (from the fortiap setup pane or dhcp)
						fortiswitch ips (from the fortiswitch setup page or dhcp)

					diagnose firewall sf-address list
					#security fabric address list

				fsso

					then on address create fsso object as specify group names
					then called them on policies which you want check credentials
						instead calling all pc and users as 2  seperated parts

					**if a user logs off and cppm receives log off confirmation, then cpps updates the fortigate fsso user list via fortimanager
					the user ip address is deleted from the dynamic fsso address, and the user is no longer be able to pass the firewall policy

			isdb (internet service db)

					predefine
					custom
						config firewall intern-service-custom
						edit x-isdb
						set reput 5
							1 known malicious site related to botnet servers, phishing site
							2 site provides high risk services such as tor, proxy, p2p
							3 nverified site
							4 reputable site from social media such as facebook, twitter
							5 known and verified safe site such as gmail, amazon, ebay

							if set 3 means our acceptable scores are 3 4 5

				reputation direction
					config firewall policy
					edit 1
					set reputation-direction source
						when we need access from wan to lan  with reputation score 3 and direction source
						means score 3 4 5 will be accessabile to clients and malware or proxies could not be reachable on wan to lan

						lan to wan used direction destination

						wan to lan used direction source

					end

		qos and shapers
			qos logic and concept
				general speaking qos
					bandwidth
						per seconds our transmission bits are 120 means 120kbps is bandwidth
						*it's different with link rate or access or clock rate

							access is duplix type and speed meaasure on links accessablities
							link rate is ingress shape or traffic shaping

							a link with 100mbps capacity wanna send 1gpbs or 0/01 seconds send intervals

							normally on physical interfaces must be higher than bandwidth or be equal

							interface serial 0/0/0
							clock-rate 64000
							no shut

							show contollers serial 0/0/0

							*usually determine by isp and cir

							*eigrp and ospf use link state and access rate as main process parameters so better force use the bandwidth instead of acccess-rate

							*recommended set same values on bandwidth and access-rate

							when port1 in fortigate  set as wan role interface means behave different
								if we have no configured queue all traffics will be forwarded as fifo queue and it's own shape
								outgoing/outbound port/shape mean from fortigae to isp or wan to isp (upload direction)
								incoming/inbound port/shape mean from isp to wan or fortigate (download direction on wan interface)

								upstream on web gui means wan to isp
								downstream means from fortigate to inside the network

								config system interface
								edit port1
								get | grep ingress
									ingress-shaping-profile
										this is automatic detection mechanism on isp side with cir
										better bypass this section

										if has slow connection must contact with isp and check ports

					jitter
						differences between each delay

						delay variation (differences on delay)

						real time protocol (rtp)
						udp and forward directly to ip

						each tool make our delay smaller make jitter too

					delay
						trip time from source to destination
						our total delay named latency

						voice normal delay > till 150 msec
							on cisco till 200 msec

						serialization delay (sd)
							router has serial interface which information will be convert to signals this converting has delay
							cause calculation

							(1500 byte * 8 bit) / 64000 bit (64kb) > 187.5 msec
							bit count divided to link speed

							is not appropriate for voice

							must use codec for voice and manage them (convert to smaller packets cause wan links are limited)

							(60*8) / 64000 > 7.5 msec

							clock-rate 		sd 155 byte  	sd 1500 byte
							100mbps 		0.01 		 	0.12			msec
							1.5mbps 		0.65 		 	8				msec
							512kbps 		2 			 	24				msec
							128kbps 		7.8 		 	93				msec
							56kbps 			17.85 		 	214				msec

							serialization delay depends on line speed and pacekt size
							sd doesn't change
							sd use codec

							higher 1 mbps wan link is okay and sd is not challenge

						propagation delay (pd)
							publishing
							distance on meter measure / speed of publication in line

							is so small delay
							qos has no effect on pd and sd

						queueing delay (qd)
							always sense them
							by default is fifo

							useful in qos

							q1 > high priority (voip will be here)
								classification and marking (marking will be dscp)

							q2 > fifo

						processing or forwarding delay (fd)
							completely recieved and reassembling data on peer
							wire speed forward
							always we have store and forward
							on switches we have fragment free and cut through

							we have no forwarding delay on calculation

						shaping delay (shd)
							our access rate is on 128k
							cir is on 64k
							so better use shape and remained packets will be queue
							on another time which has free line on bandwidth forward queued data

							shape help us to manage filling the wan links and use more bursts

							our priority is voice data this voice never goes to queue just normal traffic will be goes to queue (normal traffic might have dropping mechanism)

						network delay (nd)
							inside isp delay
							on cir and contract must set this

							on sla must check this

						sd+pd+fd+qd+shd+nd > maximum must be on 150 - 200 msec

						*minimum bandwidth and sd on this state give us real sd

						coded delay (cd)
							convert voice to special formats
							specific sample rates

						compression delay
							usually sd get shorter on hardware process also effects on qd

						more bandwidth provide less sd and less qd

					packet lost
						on fcs detect faults or noises and drop them

						ber (bit error base)
							10 pow 9
							queue size if reached 10 pow 9 menas drop is too much

						main reason is tail drop
						our processed packets were too much (huge queue size and packet count)

						queue pool is maximum length of queue example our pool queue is 50 if recieved 51 packet get drop

						if use too large queue our delay get trouble and lower packet loss

						offer use 2 queue one of them has lowest delay and smaller another one is bigger and used for data

					compression
						a tool which help us make optimized bandwidth consumption
						same part on headers will be send
						another parts will be send without headers
						details are not important

						compression might have delay cause our cpu must process the tasks

						we can also use queue as tool
						create 2 queue on device
							data queue 750k
							voice queue 250k
							------------------
							1m connection link

						call admission controll (cac)
							voice to voice
							if were more than queue space doesn't store on queue

							q2 > 250 k
								packet 1
								packet 2
								packet 3

								drop new pacekt as packet 4

					link fragment and interleaving (lfi)
						we have 2 things and put another things between them
						if recieved voice data on link cuase we changed bandwidth and priority our voice packet will be store because our last packet were too large and must join on queue
						lfi make fragment on packets so our queue will be fixed

				tos, ipp, dscp
					perhop behavior cause low speed so our classification and marking (cnm) must get optimized
					on router must make classification and marking another routers just forward it by standard codes and marks ... base on marks all routers can determine and detect what should to do

					marking is on layer 3 and ip header in tos field

					tos is 8 bit
						0000 0000 > this is best effort and normal

					ip precedense (ipp)
						use 3 first bits of tos field

						000 > 0 (low priority) (routin)
						001 > 1 (priority)
						010 > 2 (immediate)
						011 > 3 (flash)
						100 > 4 (flash override)
						101 > 5 (critical)
						110 > 6 (inter-network controll like ospf ... )
						111 > 7 (high priority) (network controll)

						must set manually

						tos > 000   0000  	0
							  ---  	----	-
							  ipp    tos	mbz (must busy)
									  |
									  |
									which one is important?
									  |
									  |
									  1 0 0 0 > minimum delay
									  0 1 0 0 > maximum through put
									  0 0 1 0 > minimum monetary cost
									  0 0 0 1 > normal service

					dscp
						tos > 0 0 0 0 0 0  	00
							  ----------- 	--
							  dscp 			tecn(explicit congestion notification)

						tos changed to dscp field name

						we have 6 bit in dynamic states
						2 pow 6 means 64 modes

						*ecn used congest avoidance
						*af (assued forwarding) divided to 2 part as --- 3 first bits use as forwarder and --- 3 second bits use as drop

						---  	--0 	--
						fwd  	drp 	ecn

						in drp part we have fixet 0

						6th bit or last bit in drop path is fixed and default

						fwd contain 0 - 7 and higher means forwarding as high priority

						drp contain 0 - 3 and higher means drop as high priority

						001 > 1   	01 > 1
						010 > 2 	00 > 0

						001 > 1 	01 0 > 1 0 > 2 >>>>> 12

						--- 	 	---  	--
						fwd 		drp 	ecn

						ecn has special applications with congestion avoidance target
						drop priority in congestion duration

											time

							125 kbps * 8 kbps > 64 kbps
								each token has 1 byte or 8000 bit

							125 kbps / 2 (intervals sed and wait) > 62.5 is time that one cycle has transmission and one cycle wait

							62.5 msec * 8 (send intervals) > 500 msec like befor but our voice packet did not wait 500 msec just
							wait 62.5 msec and it is faster than before

								token bucket
									a container which has token generator on each second
									10000 token mena saccess you send 10000 packet

									works on token bucket or bits

									imagine we use lower tokens on each portio, fill the container as it is 10000 token

									spillage waste

									if on time intervals our token buckets get empty and our links will be free so optimized model need 2 container and fill 10000 tokens between them limited storing

									our shape use these tokens on each interval and our bandwidth consumption will be optimized

				how set wred
					config firewall shaping-profile
					edit tsh-x
					set type queueing
					get
					config shape-entries
					edit 1
					get
					set limit 1000
					set red-probability 20
					set min 300
					set max 500
					
					#set drop range on packets , if send packets on minimum values (300 packets) we have no drop
					#if 300-500 packet per seconds enter the queues we have 0-20% drop (used and read from red-probability)
					#if exceed 500 means 100% drop
					
					end

				burst
					each 100 msec we have prize
					send more traffic on each 100 msec if you send traffic on normal ranges

					link 1mbps (50% quaranteed and 50% used for max bandwidth)

					--- 100msec burst --- 100msec non-burst --- 100msec burst --- 100msec non-burst 

					**used for congest prevention with speedy burst and prizing mechanism

					100msec interval of prize and bursts
						for normal bursts > 100 msec * 1 mbps * 50% = 1.000.000 * 50% = 500.000 * 0.1 second = 50.000

						50.000 bit / 8bit (to convert byte) = 6250 byte

					--- 200msec c-burst --- 200msec non-c-burst--- 200msec c-burst --- 200msec non-c-burst
					
					200msec interval of prize and c-bursts
						for normal bursts > 200 msec * 1 mbps * 50% = 1.000.000 * 50% = 1.000.000 * 0.1 second = 100.000

						100.000 bit / 8bit (to convert byte) = 12500 byte


					config firewall shaping-profile
					edit tr-shape-prof-test
					set type queuing
					set default-class-id 1
					config shaping-entries
					edit 1
					set class-id 1
					set guaranteed-bandwidth-percentage 5
					set maximum-bandwidth-percentage 50
					set burst-in-msec 100 
					#range from 0 to 2000
					
					set cburst-in-msec 200
					#range from 0 to 2000
					
					end

				example of 
					config firewall shaping-profile
					edit qa-prof
					set type queuing
					set default-class-id 30
					
					config shaping-entries
					edit 1
					set class-id 10
					set guaranteed-bandwidth-percentage 50
					set maximum-bandwidth-percentage 100
					next
					
					edit 2
					set class-id 20
					set guaranteed-bandwidth-percentage 30
					set maximum-bandwidth-percentage 60
					set red-probability 10
					next

					edit 3
					set class-id 30
					set guaranteed-bandwidth-percentage 20
					set maximum-bandwidth-percentage 50
					next
					end

			dscp match on firewall policies
				if see ef46 how behave with them (block them or access)(mainly used for specify dscp code forwarding if see anoher things except ef46 must drop them)

				 when tos-mask set on 0xfc = 1111 1100 (6bit used as dscp) will be checked dscp ip header section then analyze as mask
				 if masked section contains ef we have to do some thing
	 	
				 	dscp					tos(hex)	tos-mask(hex)	
				 	0	be (best effort)	0x00		0xfc			match only dscp 0, ignore ecn
				 	8	cs1					0x20		0xfc			match only dscp 8, ignore ecn
				 	10	af11				0x28		0xfc			match only dscp 10, ignore ecn
				 	12	af12				0x30		0xfc			match only dscp 12, ignore ecn
				 	14	af13				0x38		0xfc			match only dscp 14, ignore ecn
				 	16	cs2					0x40		0xfc			match only dscp 16, ignore ecn
				 	18	af21				0x48		0xfc			match only dscp 18, ignore ecn
				 	20	af22				0x50		0xfc			match only dscp 20, ignore ecn
				 	22	af23				0x58		0xfc			match only dscp 22, ignore ecn
				 	24	cs3					0x60		0xfc			match only dscp 24, ignore ecn
				 	26	af31				0x68		0xfc			match only dscp 26, ignore ecn
				 	28	af32				0x70		0xfc			match only dscp 28, ignore ecn
				 	30	af33				0x78		0xfc			match only dscp 30, ignore ecn
				 	32	cs4					0x80		0xfc			match only dscp 32, ignore ecn
				 	34	af41				0x88		0xfc			match only dscp 34, ignore ecn
				 	36	af42				0x90		0xfc			match only dscp 36, ignore ecn
				 	38	af43				0x98		0xfc			match only dscp 38, ignore ecn
				 	40	cs5					0xa0		0xfc			match only dscp 40, ignore ecn
				 	46	ef (voip)			0xb8		0xfc			match only dscp 46, ignore ecn (most common)
				 	48	cs6 (routing)		0xc0		0xfc			match only dscp 48, ignore ecn
				 	56	cs7					0xe0		0xfc			match only dscp 56, ignore ecn
				 		
				 	additional tos-mask values for advanced matching
				 		tos-mask (hex)	binary (8 bits)	
				 		0xff			11111111			match exact tos byte (dscp + ecn must both match)
				 		0xfc			11111100			default for dscp-only matching (ignore ecn)
				 		0xf0			11110000			match only the first 4 bits of dscp (class-based)
				 		0xe0			11100000			match only the first 3 bits of dscp (all af4x = 34,36,38)
				 		0xc0			11000000			match only the first 2 bits of dscp (rough class)
				 		0x03			00000011			match only the ecn bits (ignore dscp)

			ztna modes
				offnet	
					on internet side or out side of local network

				onnet
					local networks checcking

			ztna access proxies is most useful scenario which help us to provide access or publish web servers and services on users credential checking and verification over forticlient connection

				used for http , ssh , rdp , smb , ftp , other tcp 

				behave like reverse proxy

				to reduce overhead, you can disable access proxy encryption on the client, as some tcp protocols, like rdp, are already secure
				the tcp forwarding access proxy supports utm scanning and deep inspection for http, https, smtp, smtps, imap, imaps, pop3, pop3s, smb, and cifs

				the ssh access proxy provides some benefits to proxying ssh connections over tfap, including allowing ssh deep
				inspection, performing optional ssh host-key validation, and allowing one time user authentication to authenticate the ztna ssh access proxy connection and ssh server connection

			if used fortigate 7.2.0 or higher must used ems 7.2.0 and higher must be same version

			information check components
				forticlient endpoints provide the following information to forticlient ems when they register to the ems:
					device information (network details, operating system, model, and others)
					logged on user information
					security posture (on-net/off-net, antivirus software, vulnerability status, and others)
				
					it also requests and obtains a client device certificate from the ems ztna certificate authority (ca) when it registers to  forticlient ems
					the client uses this certificate to identify itself to the fortigate

				fortigate
					the fortigate maintains a continuous connection to the ems server to synchronize endpoint device information, including primarily:
						forticlient uid
						client certificate sn
						ems sn
						device credentials (user/domain)
						network details (ip and mac address and routing to the fortigate)

			**each ztna tag creates two firewall addresses in all vdoms on a fortigate
				one firewall address is the ip address, and the other firewall address is the mac address
				because each fortigate model has a global limit and a per-vdom limit for the maximum number of supported firewall addresses, the fortigate model determines the maximum number of ztna tags allowable by that unit, which is the maximum number of firewall address divided by two
				for each fortigate model's limit, see the maximum values table

				**when you need  ztna on you organization consider 2 values (ip and mac) for address


			verifying that the client information is synchronized to the fortigate
				diagnose endpoint record list 192.168.101.2
				#show the endpoint record list , filter by the endpoint ip address

				diagnose endpoint llscomm send ztna find-uid <uid>
				#query endpoints by client uid

				diagnose endpoint llscomm send ztna find-ip-vdom 192.168.1.1 root
				#query endpoints by the client ip-vdom pair

				diagnose wad dev query by uid <uid>
				#query from waddiagnosecommandbyuid
				
				diagnose wad dev query by ipv4 192.168.101.1
				#query from waddiagnosecommandbyip address
				
				diagnose test application fcnacd 7 
				diagnose test application fcnacd 8
				#check the forticlient nac daemon ztna and route

			by default, client certificate authentication is enabled on the access proxy, so when the https request is received the fortigate's wad process challenges the client to identify itself with its certificate. the fortigate makes a decision based on the following possibilities
				1. if the client responds with the correct certificate that the client uid and certificate sn can be extracted from
					if the client uid and certificate sn match the record on the fortigate, the client is allowed to continue with 	the ztna proxy rule processing
					
					if the client uid and certificate sn do not match the record on the fortigate, the client is blocked from 	further ztna proxy rule processing

				2. if the client cancels and responds with an empty client certificate
					if empty-cert-action is set to accept, the client is allowed to continue with ztna proxy rule processing
					if empty-cert-action is set to block, the client is blocked from further ztna proxy rule processing

						config firewall proxy-policy
						edit ztna-test
						get
						set empty-cert-action block
						#we can use accept or block

						end

				after this try clients to connect via forticlient to ems and fortigate

			**forticlient (windows) must be running 7.0.3 or later to detect smb

			**tcp forwarding access proxy supports communication between the client and the access proxy without ssl/tls encryption
				the connection still begins with a tls handshake
				the client uses the http 101 response to switch protocols and remove the https stack. further end to end communication between the client and server are encapsulated in the specified tcp port, but not encrypted by the access proxy
				this improves performance by reducing the overhead of encrypting an already secured underlying protocol, such as rdp, ssh, or ftps
				users should still enable the encryption option for end to end protocols that are insecure

			**use the following wad debugs to can capture the details about the connection as seen by the fortigate wad daemon
				notice that the http request has tls=0, indicating that the proxy connection between the client and access proxy is not encrypted

				diagnose wad debug enable category all
				diagnose wad debug enable level verbose
				diagnose debug enable
				#get /tcp?address=10.88.0.1&port=8080&tls=0 http/1.1
					tls 0 means transparent and no encryption

			ssh acccess proxy
				advantages of using an ssh access proxy instead of a tcp forwarding access proxy include
					establishing device trust context with user identity and device identity checks
					applying ssh deep inspection to the traffic through the ssh related profile
					performing optional ssh host-key validation of the server
					using one-time user authentication to authenticate the ztna ssh access proxy connection and the ssh server connection

				show full firewall ssh local-ca Fortinet_SSH_CA

				1. the remote endpoint registers to forticlient ems and receives the client certificate
				2. the remote endpoint tries to connect to the ssh access proxy. it must use the same username that is later used for access proxy authentication

				3. the fortigate challenges the endpoint with device identity validation
				4. the remote endpoint provides the ems issued certificate for device identification
				5. the fortigate challenges the endpoint with user authentication. for example, this could be done with basic or saml authentication
				6. the users enters their credentials on the remote endpoint
				7. the fortigate authenticates the user and collects the username
				8. using the fortigate's ca or the customer's ca certificate, the fortigate signs an ssh certificate and embeds the username in its principal
				9. the fortigate attempts to connect to the ssh server using the certificate authentication
				10. the ssh server verifies the authenticity of the certificate, and matches the username principal against its authorized_keys file
				11. if the username matches a record in the file, then the ssh connection is established. if no match is found, then the ssh connection fails

		protocol option
			control on alerts and detect file size and file types

			policy & objects
				protocol option
					log oversized files
					block oversized file/email (used for many protocol)
						if set 10 mb means log and block all file larger than 10mb

						iso files or video files could not be here

					it is common practice to allow larger files through without antivirus processing
					monitor the logs for the frequency of oversized file processing to determine whether or not to alter the settings for treating oversized files
					the threshold setting for oversized files and emails is located in the common options section

					comfort client
						off (no risks)
							download files then cache and checked by fortigate then if were okay forward to you if not drop and clear the file
							progress bars are empty
							but clients are protester about this

						on
							when enable this clients  see progress bars get close 100%
							but on background some files are placed in your system then last file part will be on fortigate and last part will not send to you
							after reassembling on fortigate and checking in fortios , if were healthy forward it to you if not send tcp reset and all download will be purged

					**he fortigate has a finite amount of resources to buffer and scan a file
						if a large file (such as an iso image or video file) is downloaded, this could overwhelm or exceed the fortigate's memory, especially if other large files are being downloaded at the same time
						a threshold is assigned to identify an oversize file or email
						the default is 10 mb
						the range varies per model, and the minimum is 1 mb
						any file or email over this threshold will not be processed by policies applying the antivirus security profile
						
					**if the fortigate enters conserve mode (kill some process) on a regular basis, lowering the threshold can lessen the impact of processing the files on memory
						this can increase risk, even though malware is more likely to be in smaller files

					web option
						chunked bypass
							chunked bypass is a mechanism in http 1.1 that allows a web server to start sending chunks of dynamically generated output in response to a request before actually knowing the actual size of the content
							
							for dynamically generated content, enabling chunked bypass speeds up the initial response to http requests, but the content is not held in the proxy as an entire file before proceeding

					email option
						Append signature
							**increase the performance over slower connections
							
							this option adds a plain text email signature to smtp email messages as they pass through the fortigate. 
							the message maximum is 1023 characters

							this feature works best in an environment where there is some standardization of what goes into the senders' personal signatures so that there is no duplication or contradiction of information
