				igmpv1
					host extention for ip multicast
					applied on ipv4 and works layer3
					porotocolo number is 2
					usually use ttl 1 (scope > segment)
					
					**if wanna icmpv6 must used multicast listener discovery(mld)

					multicast ip > 239.1.1.1 on application
					224.0.0.1 means all hosts (also visible on routers)
					224.0.0.2 means all routers (just routers not hosts)

					type of packets
						1- host memebership query
						2- host memebership report

					on each interval 60 seconds we have general query or host memebership query 
					destination of these are 224.0.0.1
					each client need multicast or used application on muticast listen or recieve this
					then each client need recieve data send host membership report

					on igmp version 1 we doesn't have leave mechanism and wait 180 seconds if didn't recieve report message for queries , detect leave concept
					in duration 180 seconds we recieve 3*60 seconds membership query or general query in our network

					on clients
						int fast 0/0
						ip igmp join-group 224.1.1.1
						ip igmp version 1

					on servers
						ip multicast-routing (enable multicast routing)
						int fast 0/0
						ip pim dense-mode
						ip igmp version 1

						sh ip mroute

					in normal we doesn't have multicast route

					igmp header version 1
						version 4bit
						type 3bit
						unused 8bit
						checksum 16bit
						group address 32 bit

						igmp version 1 has 2 type message 
							11 > means 0x11 and membership query
							12 > means 0x12 and membership report

					if client use unsolicited mode and join immediately didn't wait 60 seconds for query

						sh ip igmp groups

						*last system gives a report and from the set of requests from all systems in that area, a request is made again and instead of one person, everyone is involved

						sh ip igmp int fast 0/0 (say igmp is enable or not)

					in normal if set dense mode works on igmp v2

						debug ip igmp (multicast routing table (mrt))

					if transfer from multicast source to network multicast packets on 225.1.1.1 no one replied or deny it cause no one requested

						no ip routing 
						ip default-gateway 192.168.2.1
						int fast 0/0
						no sh
						ip address 192.168.2.2 255.255.255.0

					if config on igmp v1 and recieve igmp v2 could not understand

					igmp v2 hs no limitation like v1
					igmp v2 is igmp v1 iwth more tools
				>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>
				igmpv2
					invent on 1997
					0x11 > general query or membership query (each 60 seconds)
					0x12 > host membership report
					0x17 > leave message

					when pc request for multicast traffic not every pc answer or join

					igmp header version 2
						type
						version
						max response time
						checksum
						group address

					on turning back of report or response from client to multicast source doesn't need recieve packets
					0x11 send from multicast to client
					each client reply 0x12 to multicast 
					in igmpv2 use one 0x12 packet instead of many packets
					use last report

					response suppression mechanism

					when recieve general query in lan and all nodes reply report packets to make joining mechanism
					in last reported all clients use random time and set localy also different
					each node reach 0 send report 
					here instead of 0x12 use 0x16 to destination 224.1.1.1
					router and another nodes detect this event
					when another nodes detect this event didn't send report again so make suppress

					send leave 0x17 message to router just ommit one system but here router use another packet like 0x11 and name of packet is > group specify query (just send on leave mechanism) means still has users or must make tear down

					if has no user get tear down but has user must make query and report .....

					in version 2 aggregate version and type then name type on header
						v1
							type / version 		reserved 
							4bit / 4bit 		8bit

						v2
							type		max respose time 
							8bit 		8bit

					on version how could make compatibality
						in v1 use 0x11 or 0x12 in v2 use 11 or 12

						v1 
							type 0001
							version 0001
							reserved

						v2
							type 00010001
							max response time 00000001
							checksum x

						*on version 1 causse we doesn't have max res time must bypass this and check another parts

						on version 2 we just use 0x16 instead of 0x12 but for compatibality could detect 0x12
						also use 0x17 on v2 for leave

						each client use random timer for multicast group in verison 2 and 0x11
						eahc node reach faster to 0 on timer must send report and aother nodes use suppress mechansm

					url rondezvous directory (urd) >>> igmp v3lite
						cisco convert and emulate ssm and v3 igmp

					packet inside
						querier's robustness variable (qrv): 3 bits
							if this is non-zero, it contains the robustness variable value used by the sender of the query
							routers should update their robustness variable to  match the most recently received query unless the value is zero
							worked on wireless
							range 0-7 , normally used 2

						querier's query interval code (qqic): 8 bits
							this code is used to specify the query interval value (in seconds) used by the querier
							if the number is below 128, the value is used directly
							if the value is 128 or more, it is interpreted as an exponent and mantissa

				pim ssm (source specific multicast)

				>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>

				pim summary
					works with any unicast routing protocol (ospf bgp static routes)


					two operational modes
						pim dense mode (pim-dm)
						pim sparse mode (pim-sm)


					pim 
						version 
							pimv1	legacy cisco proprietary uses 224.0.0.2
							pimv2	standard (rfc 4601) uses 224.0.0.13 with ttl>1

							hello	30-second intervals 105-second hold timer


					pim dense mode (pim-dm)
						flood-and-prune
						
						initial flooding
							traffic sent to all pim neighbors
							cause use push mode

						prune process
							routers without receivers send prune messages
							till next refresh cycle state

						state refresh
							every 3 minutes to maintain topology
							also refresh prune state
						
						grafting
							rejoins pruned branches when new receivers appear
							router sends a graft message (unicast) to rejoin the tree
							upstream router responds with graft-ack and resumes forwarding
						
						assert process  > resolves duplicate forwarding on multi-access networks (losers prune their forwarding to avoid loops)
							lowest administrative distance
							lowest metric
							highest ip address
						
						prune override (lan-specific)
							on shared networks if one router sends a prune
								 routers trigger a 3-second timer before pruning
								 any router needing the traffic sends a join override during this window

							prevents premature pruning due to a single router’s request


						control messages
							hello > 30-second intervals (neighbor discovery)
							prune/graft > unicast to upstream neighbor
							state refresh > multicast to refresh prune states
							no rendezvous point (rp) > unlike pim-sm pim-dm does not use an rp
							ttl handling > pimv2 messages use ttl > 1 (unlike pimv1’s ttl=1)

						limitations
							inefficient > flooding consumes bandwidth unnecessarily
							no scalability > unsuitable for large networks (deprecated in nx-os/ios-xr)
							no source-specificity > cannot leverage igmpv3’s (s,g) joins


					pim sparse mode (pim-sm)
						unlike pim-dm's flooding approach pim-sm operates on an explicit join principle
							receivers must request traffic via igmp
							routers build distribution trees only when needed

						rendezvous point (rp)
							central coordination point for multicast groups

							is like a meeting point for multicast traffic
							sources send their data to the rp and receivers join the rp to get that data
							avoids unnecessary flooding and makes multicast efficient
									
						operational flow
							phase 1 > receiver registration
								igmp report > pim join toward rp
						
								igmp join
									host sends igmp report for group (239.1.1.1)
							
								pim join propagation
									last-hop router initiates (*g) join toward rp
									each router along path creates state and forwards join

								when a multicast source starts sending data
								its first-hop router forwards it to the rp
								the rp then distributes this traffic along a shared tree (rpt - rendezvous point tree)

							phase 2 > source registration
								first packet encapsulated in pim register to rp
								
								first packet handling
									source's dr encapsulates data in pim register messages (unicast to rp)
						
								rp decision making
									if receivers exist
										rp decapsulates and forwards down rpt
						
								no receivers
									rp sends register-stop

								when a receiver wants multicast traffic
								its last-hop router sends a pim join message toward the rp
								this builds a branch of the shared tree from the rp to the receiver
						
							phase 3 > spt switchover
								receivers establish optimal path after initial traffic
								optimization trigger
									last-hop routers detect source via rpt traffic
									initiate (s,g) join toward source
						
								path migration
									traffic shifts from rpt (*g) to spt (s,g)
									prune messages clean up unused rpt branches

								by default the last-hop router later switches to a direct path (spt) to the source for better efficiency
								the rp is no longer needed unless it’s on the shortest path

						two tree types
							shared tree (rpt g)
								rp-centric distribution

								notation (*g) (* 239.1.1.1)
								always rooted at rp
								initial path for all group traffic
							

							shortest path tree (spt s,g)
								direct source-to-receiver path

								notation (s,g) (10.1.1.10239.1.1.1)
								rooted at source
								created for traffic optimization
						
						rp discovery methods
							method				type	
							-------------------------------------------------------
							static rp			manual	simple but inflexible (lacks redundancy)
							-------------------------------------------------------
							auto-rp	cisco		uses 	224.0.1.39 announce /224.0.1.40 discovery  
								mapping agent selects rp from candidates

								mapping cache timeout 180 sec
							-------------------------------------------------------
							bsr	standard		uses 	bootstrap messages (224.0.0.13 all pim routers)
								elects bsr router via priority/ip
								distributes rp-set to all routers

								bsr election
									highest priority > highest ip
									floods bootstrap messages domain-wide

								rp selection
									candidates advertise to bsr via unicast
									bsr distributes rp-set in bootstrap messages


								rp election algorithm
									when multiple rps are available for a group

									longest prefix match
										rp advertising the most specific group range wins
										239.1.1.1/32 beats 239.0.0.0/8
						
									priority value
										lower numerical priority values are preferred

									hash
										(1103515245 * (group & mask) + 12345 xor rp-address) % 2^31
										provides deterministic rp selection
										enables load balancing across rps

									highest ip address
							-------------------------------------------------------
						
						
					advanced 
						source-specific multicast (ssm)
							uses igmpv3 to specify (s,g) channels
							operates in 232.0.0.0/8 range
							eliminates need for rp in ssm scenarios

							igmpv3 enhancements
								include mode contain join (192.168.1.50 232.1.1.1)
								exclude mode get block specific sources
								header changes placed new 0x22 report type to 224.0.0.22

							direct spt construction
								last-hop routers send (s,g) joins toward source(just use this type not more)
								no (*g) state created

							optimized forwarding
								imidiately use shortest unicast path (no rpt conversion)

							msdp incompatibility
								ssm doesn't require source discovery
								msdp becomes unnecessary in pure ssm deployments

							flag	
								st		ssm shortest path tree
								s		ssm channel
								j		join pending

						anycast rp
							provides rp redundancy using msdp
							multiple rps share same ip address
							enables fault tolerance and load balancing

						msdp (multicast source discovery protocol)
							enables inter-domain multicast by connecting pim-sm rps across different autonomous systems (ases)
							shares active source information between rps without flooding multicast traffic
							sa (source active) advertisements (on-demand source information retrieval  reduces initial sa flooding)
								source address (s)
								group address (g)
								rp address (originator)
								metric information

							inter-domain multicast support
							tcp/639 peering between rps
							requires pim-sm with anycast rp for proper operation

						bidirectional pim
							optimizes many-to-many multicast scenarios ( video conferencing)

							difference from pim-sm
								eliminates source registration process
								uses single shared tree for both directions (upstream to rp and downstream to receivers)
								no spt switchover occurs

							protocol fundamentals
								df election
									designated forwarder election on each link
									
									prevents loops without rpf checks
									winner forwards traffic bidirectionally

								rp role			
									becomes traffic meeting point (not a registration point)

							scalability
								handles thousands of sources efficiently

							simplicity
								no (s,g) state maintenance

							resource efficiency
								single tree

							df election 
								replaces rpf checks in bidirectional trees
								prevents loops by electing a single forwarder per network segment
								enables bidirectional traffic flow without source registration
								no rpf check
								workd on asm
								each 3 seconds we have election and each 2.5 sec override (prevent flapping)

								steps 
									hello messages exchange
										routers advertise bidir capability via pim hellos
										contains df election priority (default=0)

									metric comparision
										lower metric is winner
										if were same on metric checks the ip address and select biggest ip address

									winner becomes df for the rp-facing interface
									losing routers prune their interfaces

							df failure
								backup routers trigger new election
								convergence within 3 seconds

							rp failure
								requires anycast rp for redundancy
								traffic blackholing until reconvergence

							igp dependency
								metric calculation to uses unicast igp cost to rp
								ecmp handling worked on higher ip as tie breaker


					protocol interactions
						igmp 
							version matters
								igmpv2/v3 required for proper pim operation

							querier election
								lowest ip address becomes igmp querier
						

						rpf (reverse path forwarding)
							critical loop-prevention mechanism
							verifies incoming interface matches unicast routing table
							
							source address validation
							ingress interface verification
						

					design best practices
						always prefer pim-sm over pim-dm

						rp placement
							centrally located in network topology


						no multicast traffic		missing pim neighbors rp unreachable
						intermittent drops			rpf failures ttl issues
						as boundary problems		missing msdp peering

			multicast in fortigate
				fgt-1 (rp)
					set normal firewall policies on lan and wan in small scenario (no nat)

					enable multicast on network part
						enable multicast routing

								port 3 (router)
									sparse mode
									dr priority 1
									igmpv3

								port 2 (recievers)
									sparse mode
									dr priority 1
									igmpv3

					system > feature visibility > multicast policy

					239.192.20.1

					set all element stream

				on client
					udp://@239.192.20.1:1234

			**when pim is enabled, the fortigate allocates memory to manage mapping information
				the fortigate communicates with neighboring pim routers to acquire mapping information and, if required, processes the multicast traffic associated with specific multicast groups

				instead of sending multiple copies of generated ip traffic to more than one specific ip destination address, pim-enabled
				routers encapsulate the data and use a class d multicast group address (224.0.0.0 to 239.255.255.255) to forward
				multicast packets to multiple destinations
				a single stream of data can be sent because one destination address is used
				client applications receive multicast data by requesting that the traffic destined for a certain multicast group address be
				delivered to them

			multicast forward vs routing
				There is sometimes confusion between the terms forwarding and routing
				These two functions should not take place at the same time
				Multicast forwarding should be enabled when the FortiGate is in NAT mode and you want to forward multicast packets between multicast routers and receivers. However, this function should not be enabled when the FortiGate itself is operating as a multicast router, or has an applicable routing protocol that uses multicast

				config system setting
				get | grep multicast

				set multicast-forward enable
				#better disable when we have multicast routing , by default is enabled

				set multicast-ttl-notchange disable
				#our device do'nt change the ttl if enable
					not increase the ttl value for forwarded multicast packets. use this option only if packets are expiring before reaching the multicast router

				set gui-multicast-policy enable
				end

			what is the mfc
				route creation
					when a receiver joins (via igmp), fortigate creates an (,g) entry
					when a source sends traffic
					it creates an (s,g) entry

				forwarding decision
					mfc checks rpf (reverse path forwarding) to validate the source path
					traffic is replicated to outgoing vifs (lan interfaces)(behave like instace)

				aging
					entries time out if no traffic/joins are seen (default 3 minutes for (,g))

				view mfc entries
					diagnose ip multicast list
					vif 3 (port3) > source 192.168.20.200, group 239.192.20.1, outvifs 2 (port2)
						vif 3 incoming interface (rpf-validated)
						outvifs 2 outgoing interface (lan)

				flush mfc (debugging)
				diagnose ip multicast mfc-flush
					use case force reprocessing of routes after config changes
					after changing rp/pim configurations
					if routes are stale or incorrect
					new entries rebuild as receivers re-join or sources resend traffic
					clears all (s,g) and (*,g) entries
					brief traffic interruption 
					** occurs during rebuild

				manually add routes (advanced)
					diagnose ip multicast mfc-add <source> <group> <in_vif> <out_vifs>
					diagnose ip multicast mfc-add 192.168.20.200 239.192.20.1 3 2
					
					3 = incoming vif (port3)
					2 = outgoing vif (port2)

					bypassing rpf checks in labs (not recommended in production)
					testing specific flows without igmp/pim

				missing (s,g) entries check pim neighbors and rp configuration
				rpf failures
					verify unicast routes to the source/rp
				
				traffic not forwarded
					ensure multicast policies exist on fortigate

				use cse vif
					verify if traffic is correctly forwarded
					check rpf (reverse path forwarding) status
					dentify vif ids for mfc-add
					verify interface binding for multicast

				mfc versus mrib
					mfc = forwarding cache (fast path)
					mrib (multicast rib) = control plane (routes)

				ttl threshold
					use diagnose ip multicast ttl-threshold to adjust hop limits

					sso admin
						sso administrators are automatically created when the fortigate acts as a saml service provider (sp) with saml single sign-on enabled in the security fabric settings
						on the system login page, an administrator can log in with their username and password against the root fortigate acting as the identity provider (idp) in the security fabric
						after the first successful log in, this user is added to the administrators table (system > administrators under single sign-on administrator)

						the default profile selected is based on the sp settings (default admin profile)

						system > administrator > sso admin
							set vdom , user and profile

						forticloud sso
							by default, the fortigate is configured to allow administrators to log in using forticloud single sign-on
							both iam and non-iam users on the forticloud support portal are supported
							non-iam users must be the forticloud account that the fortigate is registered to

							**adding an iam user in the forticloud identity & access management (iam) guide for more information. the portal permissions for supportsite, iamportal, and fortios sso must be configured to allow portal access for administrators

							end

					password policy
						the minimum length, between 8 and 64 characters
						if the password must contain uppercase (a, b, c) and/or lowercase (a, b, c) characters
						if the password must contain numbers (1, 2, 3)
						if the password must contain special or non-alphanumeric characters: !, @, #, $, %, ^, &, *, (, and ) where the password applies (admin or ipsec or both)
						the duration of the password before a new one must be specified
						the minimum number of unique characters that a new password must include

						**Using secure passwords is vital for preventing unauthorized access to your FortiGate When changing the password, consider the following to ensure better security
							Do not use passwords that are obvious, such as the company name, administrator names, or other obvious words or phrases
							
							Use numbers in place of letters like passw0rd
							
							Administrator passwords can be up to 64 characters
							
							Include a mixture of numbers, symbols, and upper and lower case letters
							
							Use multiple words together, or possibly even a sentence, correcthorsebatterystaple
							
							Use a password generator
							
							Change the password regularly and always make the new password unique and not a variation of the existing 
							password , do not change from password to password1
							
							Make note of the password and store it in a safe place away from the management computer, in case you forget it; or ensure at least two people know the password in the event one person becomes unavailable Alternatively, have two different admin logins

							config system password-policy
							set status enable
							set apply-to {admin-password | ipsec-preshared-key}
							set minimum-length <8-128>
							set min-lower-case-letter <0-128>
							set min-upper-case-letter <0-128>
							set min-non-alphanumeric <0-128>
							set min-number <0-128>
							set min-change-characters <0-128>
							set expire-status enable
							set expire-day <1-999>
							set reuse-password disable
							end

							**better used ad and ldap here

					public key ssh access
						ssh
							pros		
								more secure (higher complexity)
								restricts logon to hosts that have the private key
								never sent to the fortigate
								can add a password in addition to the key
								
							cons
								more complex to implement
								the private key is only as secure as the system storing it
								more complicated to train users and administrators to use keys

						password
							pros
								easy to remember
								easy to update
								can log in from any system

							cons
								might be guessable or brute forced
								could be reused and compromised on another system
								might be stored in plain text on an authenticating device (this does not apply to fortigates)
								can be phished or observed if written down

						*key-pair authentication is often implemented when connecting to the fortigate without any human interaction, such as when using a script
						the script can leverage existing mechanisms to secure private keys, instead of trying to develop a way to securely store a username and password

						used putty generator
							set key lenght
							normally used rsa
							after create keys , clear comment section (kust need key no more)
							save public and private keys

							config system admin
							edit adminx
							set ssh-public-key1 <ssh-rsa> <keys>
							end

			**in a custom upgrade, the configuration backups are saved when the administrator schedules the upgrade
			if the scheduled upgrade occurs after further configuration changes are made, the latest changes will not be saved in a new backup configuration file

			fortios image signing and verification
				official fortios firmware images are signed by the fortinet ca
				the bios checks the validity of an image when it is uploaded to the device
				if the image is not signed by the fortinet ca, warning messages appear in the gui in several locations and in the cli when the uploaded firmware fails the signature validation

				execute restore image tftp fgt 100f-v7-build0197-fortinet.out 192.168.20.200
				this operation will replace the current firmware version!
				do you want to continue? (y/n) y
					fortinet cannot verify the authenticity of this firmware and therefore
					there may be a risk that the firmware contains code unknown to fortinet.

				**firmware images are signed and the signature is attached to the code as it is built. when upgrading an image, the running os will generate a signature and compare it with the signature attached to the image
				if the signatures do not match, the new os will not load

				to check firmware we have reboot device
					if verified the os or firmware then iinstall and continue the procedure of upgrade then restart

					connect to the cli using an rj-45 to usb (or db-9) or null modem cable

					ensure that the tftp server is running

					copy the new firmware image file to the root directory on the tftp server

					ensure that the fortigate unit can connect to the tftp server using the execute ping command

				restore from usb

					can upload a new version of the fortios firmware to a separate partition in the fortigate memory for later upgrade

						do you want to continue? (y/n)
							type y. the fortigate unit uploads the firmware image file, then a message similar to the following is shown
								get image from tftp server ok.	
								check image ok.
								this operation will downgrade the current firmware version!
	
								do you want to continue? (y/n)
									type y. the fortigate unit downgrades to the old firmware version and restarts. this process takes a few minutes.

			auxiliary session 
			    is a feature helps manage return traffic paths
			    especially in scenarios inoling policy-based routing and asymmetric routing
			    it allows the fortigate to create separate sessions for different traffic paths
			    improing traffic handling and performance

			    asymmetric routing
			        in networks where traffic may take different paths to and from a destination (asymmetric routing)
			        auxiliary sessions help ensure that return traffic is properly managed this is crucial for maintaining the state of connections and ensuring that packets are routed correctly

			    policy-based routing
			        when using policy-based routing different types of traffic may be directed through different interfaces or paths
			        based on predefined policies auxiliary sessions allow the firewall to track these sessions separately ensuring that the correct return path is used

			    how it works
			        session creation
			            when a session is initiated the fortigate creates an auxiliary session to track the return path this session is separate from the main session allowing for more granular control oer traffic

			        session management
			            the firewall can manage these auxiliary sessions independently which helps in scenarios where the main session might not be able to handle return traffic due to routing policies or network changes

			    adantages
			        improed performance
			            managing return traffic more effectiely auxiliary sessions can enhance oerall network performance and reduce latency

			        enhanced security
			            proper session management helps in maintaining the integrity of connections reducing the risk of session hijacking or other security threats

			      
			    where we can use it
			        in data center enironments where multiple paths exist for redundancy and load balancing auxiliary sessions can help maintain efficient traffic flow

			        complex networks for organizations with complex network topologies such as those using multiple isps or pns auxiliary sessions proide a way to manage traffic effectiely


			    config system session-ttl
			        set default 3600
			    end
			     
			        client initiates connection a client from the internet sends a request to the serer behind fortigate a the request goes through isp 1

			        fortigate a creates a session fortigate a creates a session for this incoming traffic and tracks it

			        return traffic management when the serer responds fortigate a uses the auxiliary session to manage the return traffic if the return traffic needs to go through fortigate b (policy-based routing decision) 
			        the auxiliary session ensures that the response is routed correctly

			        session continuity the auxiliary session allows fortigate a to maintain the state of the connection ensuring that the return traffic is handled properly even if it takes a different path

			    *both fortigate deices are running the same firmware ersion

			    cpu processing
			        in many fortigate models the cpu is responsible for managing session states including auxiliary sessions 
			        includes creating tracking and terminating sessions based on the traffic flow and routing policies

			        when auxiliary sessions are enabled the cpu handles the logic for maintaining the state of these sessions especially in scenarios inoling complex routing or asymmetric traffic flows

			    npu processing
			       fortigate deices equipped with npus are designed to offload certain types of processing from the cpu to enhance performance 
			       npus are optimized for handling high speed packet processing and can manage sessions more efficiently in high throughput enironments

			       in scenarios where npus are utilized they can handle the data plane operations including the forwarding of packets based on established sessions 
			       allows the cpu to focus on control plane tasks such as session management and policy enforcement

			    without auxiliary sessions
			        if auxiliary sessions are not utilized all session management and processing would typically be handled by the cpu 
			        this could lead to increased cpu load especially in enironments with high traffic olumes or complex routing scenarios

			        the absence of auxiliary sessions may also result in less efficient handling of return traffic particularly in asymmetric routing situations where the return path differs from the outgoing path

			    *benefits of using auxiliary sessions
			      improed traffic management 
			        auxiliary sessions help manage return traffic more effectiely especially in scenarios inoling asymmetric routing where the return path differs from the outgoing path 
			        ensures that packets are routed correctly and efficiently

			      enhanced performance 
			        by allowing the firewall to create separate sessions for different traffic paths auxiliary sessions can reduce the load on the cpu 
			        can lead to better performance especially in high throughput enironments

			      increased reliability 
			        in multi-isp setups auxiliary sessions can proide redundancy and failoer capabilities 
			        if one path fails the firewall can manage sessions oer an alternatie path without dropping connections

			      better session state management 
			        auxiliary sessions maintain the state of connections more effectiely which is crucial for applications that require consistent session states (oip ideo conferencing)

			      policy-based routing support 
			        they work well with policy-based routing (pbr) allowing for more granular control oer how traffic is routed based on arious criteria such as source ip destination ip or serice type

			      security enhancements 
			        by managing sessions more effectiely auxiliary sessions can help reduce the risk of session hijacking and other security threats as the firewall can track and control the state of connections more accurately
			      
			    *disadantages of using auxiliary sessions
			      complexity in configuration 
			        configuring auxiliary sessions can add complexity to the firewall setup network administrators need to understand the implications of enabling this feature and how it interacts with existing routing and security policies

			      resource utilization 
			        while auxiliary sessions can offload some processing from the cpu they still require resources to manage in enironments with limited hardware capabilities this could lead to resource contention

			      potential for misconfiguration 
			        if not configured correctly auxiliary sessions can lead to routing issues or unexpected behaior in traffic flow 
			        could result in dropped packets or degraded performance

			      monitoring and troubleshooting challenges 
			        monitoring and troubleshooting auxiliary sessions may require additional tools or expertise understanding how these sessions interact with other network components can be challenging

			      dependency on hardware 
			        the effectieness of auxiliary sessions may depend on the specific fortigate model and its hardware capabilities lower end models may not benefit as much from this feature compared to higher end models with more robust npus

			    packet processing without auxiliary sessions
			      cpu handling 
			        when auxiliary sessions are disabled 
			            all session management and packet processing are handled by the cpu 
			            includes creating tracking and terminating sessions for all traffic flows

			                session creation establishing a session for the packet which includes checking the session table applying 
			                
			                security policies and determining the appropriate action (allow deny)
			               
			                policy enforcement applying firewall rules and other security policies to the packet
			               
			                routing decisions making decisions about where to forward the packet based on the routing table and session state
			        
			        the cpu processes packets based on the established session state which can lead to increased cpu load especially in high traffic enironments or complex routing scenarios

			      npu inolement 
			        in fortigate deices equipped with npus 
			            npu is designed to offload certain types of packet processing from the cpu this includes high speed packet forwarding and some security functions

			                packet forwarding once the cpu has established the session and made the necessary decisions the npu can take oer the forwarding of packets for that session the npu is optimized for high speed packet processing and can handle large olumes of traffic efficiently

			                offloading the npu offloads certain tasks from the cpu allowing the firewall to handle more traffic without oerloading the cpu this is particularly important in high throughput enironments

			        even if auxiliary sessions are disabled the npu can still be utilized for packet forwarding once the session is established by the cpu 
			        while the initial session management occurs on the cpu the actual forwarding of packets can be handled by the npu for efficiency

			    transition from cpu to npu
			        initial processing when a packet arries the cpu processes it to determine the session state and apply any releant security policies this includes checking the session table applying firewall rules and making routing decisions
			      
			        forwarding once the session is established and the cpu has determined how to handle the packet the npu can take oer the forwarding of subsequent packets for that session this allows for high speed processing and reduces the load on the cpu

			    differences in mechanism
			        session management 
			           without auxiliary sessions 
			             the cpu handles all aspects of session management including tracking the state of connections this can lead to increased cpu load especially in scenarios with high traffic or complex routing

			             once a session is established the npu can take oer forwarding packets but the cpu remains responsible for managing the session state
			           
			           with auxiliary sessions 
			             auxiliary sessions allow for better management of return traffic and can help reduce the cpu load by creating separate sessions for different traffic paths
			           
			             the cpu still initiates the session but the auxiliary session mechanism allows for more efficient handling of traffic particularly in asymmetric routing scenarios
			        
			        performance 
			           without auxiliary sessions the reliance on the cpu for all session management can lead to performance bottlenecks especially under heay load the cpu may become a limiting factor in throughput

			           with auxiliary sessions by optimizing how sessions are managed auxiliary sessions can improe oerall performance and reduce latency as they allow for more efficient routing of return traffic
			        
			        resource utilization 
			           without auxiliary sessions the cpu is heaily utilized for both session management and packet processing which can lead to resource contention and potential performance degradation

			           with auxiliary sessions the workload can be distributed more effectiely allowing the npu to handle more of the packet forwarding thus freeing up cpu resources for other tasks

			    diagram packet processing steps
			       incoming packet 
			        cpu          
			        (initial check)  
			               check session table
			               create session (if new)
			               apply security policies
			               make routing decision
			              
			        session exists?        
			               yes
			        npu          
			        (forward packet)  

			        forward packet
			              
			        outgoing packet  
			    
			    incoming packet a packet arries at the fortigate firewall from the network

			    cpu (initial check) 
			       the cpu performs an initial check on the packet
			       it checks the session table to see if a session already exists for this packet
			       if the session does not exist the cpu creates a new session applies security policies and makes a routing decision

			    session exists? 
			       the cpu checks if a session already exists for the incoming packet
			       if a session exists the packet is forwarded to the npu for processing

			    npu (forward packet) 
			       the npu takes oer the forwarding of the packet it is optimized for high speed packet processing and can handle large olumes of traffic efficiently

			    outgoing packet the packet is forwarded out of the fortigate firewall to its destination

			    diagram with auxiliary sessions
			       incoming packet  

			        cpu          
			        (initial check)  
			            check session table
			            create session (if new)
			            apply security policies
			            make routing decision
			                
			       session exists?  
			           yes
			              
			       auxiliary session? 
			           yes
			            
			        npu          
			        (forward packet)  
			    
			        forward packet
			            
			       outgoing packet  
			    
			      steps explained with auxiliary sessions

			    incoming packet a packet arries at the fortigate firewall from the network

			    cpu (initial check) the cpu performs the same initial checks as before

			    session exists? 
			        the cpu checks if a session already exists for the incoming packet

			    auxiliary session? 
			        if a session exists the cpu checks if it is an auxiliary session if it is the packet is forwarded to the npu for processing

			    npu (forward packet) the npu takes oer the forwarding of the packet optimizing performance

			    outgoing packet the packet is forwarded out of the fortigate firewall to its destination

			    **in fortios 6.0 and earlier, the auxiliary session feature is not supported
			    in fortios 6.2.0 to 6.2.2, the auxiliary session feature is permanently enabled
			    in fortios 6.2.3 and later, the auxiliary session feature is disabled by default, and can be enabled if required

			    **when enabling auxiliary sessions, consider the impact of routing in both traffic directions
			    in topologies such as sd-wan hub and spoke or advpn deployments, the symmetry of the return traffic is important for maintaining the stability of the session. it is expected that the spoke selects the outbound interface and path, and the other nodes obey and reply symmetrically
			    it is recommended to disable auxiliary in these scenarios, and others where incoming and return traffic symmetry is expected

			    **create table to prevent session hijaking

			    cpu on init state is  high, after first time negotiation goes to npu (with aux or without aux could be manage with npu)

			    config system seting
			    get | grep aux
			    set auxiliary-session enable
			    #default is enable, recommended disable this and just used symmetric

			    set asymmetric-routing enable
			    end

			    scenarios
			    	1 - return traffic returns on the original outgoing interface
			    		a session is established between port1 and port3. when the return traffic hits port3
			    			auxiliary sessions disabled
			    				the reply to the client egresses on the original incoming interface, port1. if policy routes or sd-wan rules are configured, the next hop gateway is applied if the output device is the same as the original incoming interface

			    			auxiliary sessions enabled
			    				the reply to the client egresses on the best route in the routing table
			    					if the best route is port1, then it will egress on port1
			    					if the best route is port2, then it will egress on port2
			    				
			    				if policy routes or sd-wan rules are configured, they must be matched to determine the egress interface
			    				if both are configured, policy routes have higher priority

			    	2 - return traffic returns on an interfaces other than the original outgoing interfaces
			    		a session is established between port1 and port3. when the return traffic hits port4
			    			auxiliary sessions disabled
			    				the session is dirtied and then gets refreshed, and interfaces on the session are updated
			    				if there is a high traffic volume or flapping between the interfaces, the cpu usage increases

			    			auxiliary sessions enabled
			    				an auxiliary session is created for the existing session, and traffic returns to the client as normal on the auxiliary session

			    	3 - incoming traffic enters on an interfaces other than the original incoming interfaces
			    		a session is established between port1 and port3. when the incoming traffic hits port2
			    			auxiliary sessions disabled:

			    			the session is dirtied and then gets refreshed, and interfaces on the session are updated.

			    			auxiliary sessions enabled:

			    			an auxiliary session is created for the existing session, and traffic is forwarded to the server as normal on the auxiliary
			    			session.

			    	4 - the routing table is changed
			    			a session has been established between port1 and port3, when a new route on port4 is updated as the route to the server
			    			auxiliary sessions disabled
			    				as long as there is a route to the destination, the session will not be dirtied or refreshed. even though there is a better
			    				route, traffic continues on the original path between port1 and port3

			    			auxiliary sessions enabled
			    				the session is dirtied and then gets refreshed, and interfaces on the session are updated

			    	npu_state=0x000400
			    	npu info: flag=0x91/0x00, offload=8/0, ips_offload=0/0, epid=129/0, ipid=142/0,
			    	vlan=0x0017/0x0000
			    	vlifid=142/0, vtag_in=0x0017/0x0000 in_npu=1/0, out_npu=1/0, fwd_en=0/0, qid=4/0
			    	total reflect session num: 1

			    	state = dirty
			    	#flap or ..

			    diagnose sys session list
			    	npu info: flag=0x91/0x81, offload=8/8
			    	npu info: flag=0x91/0x00, offload=8/0

			    npu offloading session
			    	when the auxiliary session feature is disabled, there is always one session
			    	if the incoming or return interface changes, the fortigate marks the session as dirty and updates the session's interfaces
			    	this cannot be done by the npu, so the session is not offloaded to the npu, and is processed by the cpu instead
			    	if equal-cost multi-path (ecmp) causes the interface to keep changing, then it will use significant cpu resources

			    	when the auxiliary session feature is enabled and the incoming or return interface changes, it creates an auxiliary session, and all traffic can continue to be processed by the npu

			trusted platform module support
				on supported fortigate hardware devices, the trusted platform module (tpm) can be used to protect your password and key against malicious software and phishing attacks
				the dedicated module hardens the fortigate by generating, storing, and authenticating cryptographic keys
				to help prevent tampering, the chip is soldered on the motherboard to reduce the risk of data transaction interceptions from attackers
				by default, the tpm is disabled
				to enable it, you must set the 32 hexadecimal digit master-encryption-password which encrypts sensitive data on the fortigate using aes128-cbc

				with the password, tpm generates a 2048-bit primary key to secure the master-encryption-password through rsa-2048 encryption. the master-encryption-password protects the data
				the primary key protects the master-encryption-password

				the tpm module does not encrypt the disk drive of eligible fortigates
				
				the primary key binds the encrypted configuration file to a specific fortigate unit and never leaves the tpm
				when backing up the configuration, the tpm uses the primary key to encrypt the master-encryption-password in the
				configuration file. when restoring a configuration that includes a tpm protected master-encryption-password
				
				if tpm is disabled, then the configuration cannot be restored
				if tpm is enabled but has a different master-encryption-password than the configuration file, then the configuration cannot be restored
				if tpm is enabled and the master-encryption-password is the same in the configuration file, then the configuration can be restored
				
				passwords and keys that can be encrypted by the master-encryption-key include
					alert email user's password
					bgp and other routing related configurations
					external resource
					fortiguard proxy password
					fortitoken/fortitoken mobile's seed
					ha password
					ipsec pre-shared key
					link monitor, server side password
					local certificate's private key
					local, ldap. radius, fsso, and other user category related passwords
					modem/pppoe
					nst password
					ntp password
					sdn connector, server side password
					snmp
					wireless security related password
				
				in ha configurations, each cluster member must use the same master-encryption-key so that the ha cluster can form and its members can synchronize their configurations

				diagnose hardware test info
				diagnose hardware deviceinfo tpm
				diagnose tpm
				
				config system global
				set private-data-encryption enable
				end

				mib
					fortinet-core-mib.mib
						the fortinet core mib includes all system configuration and trap information that is common to all fortinet products
						your snmp manager requires this information to monitor fortinet device settings and receive traps from the fortigate snmp agent

					fortinet-fortigate-mib.mib
						the fortigate mib includes all system configuration information and trap information that is specific to fortigate units
						your snmp manager requires this information to monitor fortigate settings and receive traps from the fortigate snmp agent

					rfc-1213 (mib ii)
						the fortigate snmp agent supports mib ii groups with the following exceptions
							no support for the egp group from mib ii (rfc 1213, section 3.11 and 6.10)
							protocol statistics returned for mib ii groups (ip/icmp/tcp/udp/etc.) do not accurately capture all fortinet traffic activity
							more accurate information can be obtained from the information reported by the fortinet mib

					rfc-2665 (ethernet-like mib)
						the fortigate snmp agent supports ethernet-like mib information fortigate snmp does not support for the dot3tests (loopback ip) and dot3errors (crc check) groups

					next
					end

			fortigate encryption algorithm cipher suites
				fortigates use ssl/tls encryption for https and ssh administrative access, and ssl vpn remote access
				when establishing an ssl/tls or ssh connection, you can control the encryption level and the ciphers that are used in order to control the security level

				http
					config system global
					set strong-crypto  enable
					set admin-https-ssl-versions tlsv1-2
					#tlsv1-1 , tlsv1-3

					set admin-https-ssl-ciphersuites 
					set admin-https-ssl-banned-ciphers
					end

				**when strong encryption is enabled, only tls 1.2 and tls 1.3 are allowed
					if strong encryption is then disabled, tls 1.1 has to be manually enabled
	
					setting admin-https-ssl-ciphersuites controls which cipher suites are offered in tls 1.3. tls 1.2 and lower are not affected by this 	command
	
					to disable all tls 1.3 cipher suites, remove tls1-3 from admin-https-ssl-versions
	
					setting admin-https-ssl-banned-ciphers controls which cipher technologies will not be offered for tls 1.2 and lower

				ssh
					config system global
					set admin-ssh-v1 enable
					set strong-crypto enable
					set ssh-enc-algo
					end

				ssl vpn
					config vpn ssl setting
					set algorithm high
					#mid , low

					set ssl-max-proto-ver tls1-3
					#tls1-0 , tls1-1 , tls1-2  

					set ssl-min-proto-ver tls1-3
					#tls1-0 , tls1-1 , tls1-2  

					set ciphersuite TLS-AES-128-GCM-SHA256 
					#TLS-AES-256-GCM-SHA384 , TLS-CHACHA20-POLY1305-SHA256 , TLS-AES-128-CCM-SHA256 , TLS-AES-128-CCM-8-SHA256
					
					end

					cipher suites (ciphersuite) can only be selected when the ssl maximum version is tls 1.3

					when the ssl vpn security level (algorithm) is set to high, only high levels are allowed. when it is set to medium, high and medium levels are allowed. when it is set to low, any level is allowed

					the strong encryption (strong-crypto) command has no effect on the ssl vpn encryption level or ciphers

			fortiguard
				fortiguard services can be purchased and registered to your fortigate unit
				the fortigate must be connected to the internet in order to automatically connect to the fortiguard distribution network (fdn) to validate the license and download fdn updates
					antivirus (av)
					intrusion protection service (ips)
					application control
					antispam
					web filtering
					web application firewall (waf)
				
				av and ips packages are signed by the fortinet ca, and automatic updates will validate whether a package is signed

				immediately download updates
					the option can be enabled on 2u and larger hardware models when the fortiguard are servers are conneced in anycast mode
					the fortigate forms a secure, persistent connection with fortiguard to get notifications of new updates through an https connection
					the fortigate uses the fds_notify daemon to wait for the notification, then makes another connection to the fortiguard server to download the updates

				improve ips quality
					enable to send information to the fortiguard servers when an attack occurs
					this can help keep the fortiguard database current as attacks evolve, and improve ips signatures

				antivirus pup/pua
					enable antivirus grayware checks for potentially unwanted applications

					end

						**proxy tunneling is supported only for registration, av, and ips updates
						for fortigate virtual machines, proxy tunneling can also be used for license validation

						**for web filtering or spam filtering, udp protocol is used on ports 53 or 8888
						udp protocol traffic cannot be directed over a proxy server, even if you are using versions of fortios that support web filtering over port 443

						in a closed network without direct internet connection for web filtering or spam filtering, you can use fortimanager as a fortiguard server
						fortimanager supports proxy for both updates and rating, and fortios retrieves its updates and ratings through fortimanager

				sending malware statistics to fortiguard
					fortigate devices periodically send encrypted antivirus, ips, botnet ip list, and application control statistics to fortiguard
					included with these data is the ip address and serial number of the fortigate, and the country that it is in
					this information is never shared with external parties

					shodan.io
	
					the malware statistics are used to improve various aspects of fortigate malware protection. for example, antivirus data
					allow fortiguard to determine what viruses are currently active

					signatures for those viruses are kept in the active av signature database that is used by multiple fortinet products
					inactive virus signatures are moved to the extended av signature database 
					when events for inactive viruses start appearing in the malware data, the signatures are moved back into the av signature database
	
					the fortigate and fortiguard servers go through a 2-way ssl/tls 1.2 authentication before any data is transmitted
					the certificates used in this process must be trusted by each other and signed by the fortinet ca server
					the fortigate only accepts data from authorized fortiguard severs

					fortinet products use dns to find fortiguard servers and periodically update their fortigate server list
					all other servers are provided by a list that is updated through the encrypted channel
	
					malware statistics are accumulated and sent every 60 minutes by default
	
						**can we used re-surface or active scanning on ips signatures and attacks
						**global threat reports  generated from all diagnostics information on fortiguard

					config system global
					set fds-statistics enable
					set fds-statistics-period 60
					#minutes

					end
	
					the submission of malware data is in accordance with the fortinet privacy policy
					there is no sensitive or personal information included in these submissions. only malware statistics are sent
					fortinet uses the malware statistics collected in this manner to improve the performance of the fortigate services and to display statistics on the fortinet support website for customers reaistered fortigate devices
					fortinet may also publish or share statistics or results derived from this malware data with  various audiences
					the malware statistics shared in this way do not include any customer data

						connection flow
							fgt > server
								ssl/tls client hello with ocsp status request
							
							server > fgt
								server hello with certificate and ocsp status
								fortigate evaluates the server response:
									if abort conditions are met, the ssl handshake is aborted
									if pass, the ssl handshake continues to completion
								
							fgt > server
								client certificate, key information

							server > fgt
								certificate verify, finished
							
							fgt > server
								finished

							server > fgt
								encrypted communication

							**abort conditions 
								the cn in the server's certificate does not match the domain name resolved from the dns
								the ocsp status is not good
								the issuer-ca is revoked by the root-ca

				iot detection service
					internet of things (lot) detection is a subscription service that allows fortigate to detect unknown devices in fortiguard
					that are not detected by the local device database (cidb)
					when the service is activated, fortigate can send device information to the fortiguard collection server
					when a new device is detected, fortigate queries the results from the fortiguard query for more information about the device

					this feature requires an lot detection service license

					the fortigate device must be
						registered with forticare
						connected to an anycast fortiguard server

					enable device detection on an interface
					fortigate uses the interface to detect device traffic flow
					upon detecting traffic from an unknown device, fortigate sends the device data to the fortiguard collection server
					the collection server returns data about the new device to the fortiguard query server
					if the device signature does not appear in the local device database (cidb) or some fields are not complete, fortigate queries fortiguard for more information about the device

					dashboard > users & devices and expand the device inventory widget

					diagnose cid sigs disable
					#disable the local device database in order to force all queries to go to fortiguard
					
					diagnose debug application iotd -1
					diagnose debug enable
					#enable iotd debugs

					diagnose user device list
					#show  iot devices

				fortiap query to fortiguard lot service to determine device details
					a fortiap collects packets from devices and queries fortiguard with the help of the fortigate
					device detection results are reported back to the fortigate where this information is displayed
						dhcp , mac  http (agents like edge ,safarii....)

					querying the fortiguard service requires an lot detection service license

				licensing in air-gap environments
					in the operational technology industry, industrial equipment is critical and must not be connected to the internet

					the equipment is still required to be protected by a firewall in this air-gap environment
					without a gateway to fortiguard in air-gap environments, fortiguard packages, such as antivirus and ips, must be manually uploaded to the fortigate
					fortigate licenses can be downloaded from forticloud and uploaded manually to the fortigate

					manual licensing for air-gap environments is supported only on fortigate hardware appliances, for both rugged and non-rugged models running fortios 7.2.0 or later
					
					**manual licensing is currently not supported on fortigate virtual machine (vm) appliances

			certificate
				fortios leverages certificates in multiple areas, such as vpns, administrative access, and deep packet inspection
				this section contains topics about uploading certificates and provides examples of how certificates may be used to encrypt
				and decrypt communications, and represent the identity of the fortigate
				this sections assumes the reader has a high level understanding of the public key infrastructure (pki) system, particularly how entities leverage trusted certificate authorities (cas) to verify the authenticating party, and how public and private certificate keys work to secure communications

				after add ca cert on frotigate we have generate certificate signing request (csr) to match all negotiation between fortigate and ca server , so csr will be signed on ca server then could be verified on fabric
					execute vpn certificate local generate cmp <certificate name> <key size> <server> <path> <server_certificate> <auth_certificate> <user> <password> <subject> [sans] [options]
					#certificate management protocol version 2 , used for automatic cert load(approve) and let's encrypt , used on pki , used on large scale companies

					execute vpn certificate local generate default-ssl-ca
					#ssl inspection on built-in ca ssl certs

					execute vpn certificate local generate default-ssl-key-certs

					execute vpn certificate local generate default-ssl-serv-key

					execute vpn certificate local generate ec <certificate_name> <curve_name> <subject> <country> <state/province> <city> <organization> <ou> <email> [sans] [options]

					execute vpn certificate local generate rsa <certificate_name> <key_size> <subject> <country> <state/province> <city> <organization> <ou> <email> [sans] [options]

				remote ca and certificate
					on fortigate used for authentication and inspection not more (dpi or ..) , these parameters used on fortinet fabric interconnectivities

					remote certificates are public certificates and contain only the public key
					they are used to identify a remote device
						when configuring your fortigate for saml authentication with the fortigate as an identity provider (idp), you can optionally specify the service provider (sp) certificate. however, when configuring your fortigate as a sp, you must specify the certificate used by the idp. both these certificates can be uploaded to the fortigate as a remote certificate, since the private key is not necessary for its implementation

				import
					although import is often used in conjunction with a csr, you may upload a certificate to the fortigate that was generated on its own

					this is typical of wildcard certificates ( *. domain.tld) where the same certificate is used across multiple devices (fgt.domain.tld, faz.domain.tld, ...), but may be used for individual certificates so long as the information provided to the signing ca matches that of the fortigate

					when selecting import, there are four options: local certificate, ca certificate, remote certificate, and crl

					any certificate uploaded to a vdom is only accessible to that vdom
					any certificate uploaded to the global vdom is giobally accessible by all vdoms
					a signed certificate that is created using a csr that was generated by the fortigate does not include a private key, and can be imported to the fortigate from a tftp file server
	
						execute vpn certificate local import tftp <file_name> <server_address> <cert_type> [password]
	
						config vpn certificate local 
						#ca , remote , ocsp-server ,crl
						#vdom disable

						config certificate local 
						#ca ,remote ,crl
						#vdom enable
	
				local certificate
					local certificates are used by the fortigate to identify itself, or a service it provides, such as https administrative access, ssl vpn user portal, or virtual server load balancing where the fortigate masquerades as the destination server 

					pkcs#12 certificates
						this option takes a specific certificate file type that contains the private key
						the certificate will be encrypted and a password must be supplied with the certificate file

					certificates
						this option is intended for certificates that were generated without using the fortigate's csr since the certificate private key is being uploaded, a password is required
							certificate file and key file (typically .cer and .pem)
							certificate and key bundle file (typically .pfx)

					automated
						this option allows you to configure the automated certificate management environment (acme), which allows you to request and use trusted certificates signed by let's encrypt

				acme certificate support
					the automated certificate management environment (acme), as defined in rfc 8555, is used by the public let's encrypt certificate authority (https://letsencrypt.org) to provide free ssl server certificates

					the fortigate can be configured to use certificates that are managed by let's encrypt, and other certificate management that use the acme protocol
					the server certificates can be used for secure administrator log in to the fortigate

					acme certificates do not support loopback interfaces

					the fortigate must have a public ip address and a hostname in dns (eqdn) that resolves to the public ip address.
					the configured acme interface must be public facing so that the fortigate can listen for acme update requests
						it must not have any vips, or port forwarding on port 80 (http) or 443 (https)

					the subject alternative name (san) field is automatically filled with the fortigate dns hostname
						it cannot be edited, wildcards cannot be used, and multiple sans cannot be added

	vm
		system > fortiguard

			license status
				valid
					vm can connect and validate the license against a fortimanager or fortiguard server. all features are available

				warning
					vm cannot connect and validate against a fortimanager or fortiguard server
					a check is made against how many days the warning status is continuous
					if the number is less than 30 days, the status does not change

				invalid
					vm cannot connect and validate against a fortimanager or fortiguard server
					a check is made against how many days the warning status is continuous
					if the number is 30 days or more, the status changes to invalid
					gui access is restricted until a valid license is uploaded
					firewall policies do not work 
					fortiguard downloads are unavailable

				pending
					temporary state where the vm attempts to validate its license
					reasons for having a warning or invalid status include
						the network environment does not allow fortigate-vm to connect to the fortiguard server
						the license may be expired. check the expiration date for evaluation or term-based licenses
						another vm has been already validated with fortiguard using the same license see vm license activation for details about duplicated vm instances

				diagnose debug vm-print-license
				diagnose hardware sysinfo vm full
				#if see status code 200 means  ok, valid license , if see 401 means warning on license
					valid
						0 - invalid
						1 - valid

					status
						0 - startup
						1 - success
						2 - warning
						3 - error
						4 - invalid copy (duplicate license on fabric)
						5 - eval expired
						6 - grace period. for fortiflex, there is a two-hour grace period to begin passing traffic upon retrieving the license from forticare

					code
						2xx, 3xx - success
						200 - valid
						202- accepted (treated as correct response code)
						4xx - error
						400 - expired
						401 - duplicate
						5xx - warning
						500 - warning
						502- invalid. cannot connect to fortiguard distribution servers
						6xx - evaluation license expired
						other codes - error

			license types
				fortigate-vm offers perpetual licensing (normal series and v-series) and annual subscription licensing (s-series)
				skus are based on the number of vcpus (1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, or unlimited)
					fgtvme.... (is v-series)
					fgvm ...s (is s-series)

					v-series and normal , pay annual and one time then resubmission happen
						normal license has vdom option on it also neeed seperated single vcpu with payment

						v-series used seperated values and calculation on vdom

				the fortiflex program allows qualified enterprise and mssp customers to create as many vm entitlements as required

				resource consumption is based upon predefined points that are calculated on a daily basis

				licensing and support
					v-series and normal
						the vm base is perpetual
						you must separately contract support services on an annual basis

					s-series
						single annually contracted sku that contains a vm base and a forticare service bundle
						four support service bundle types are available
							only forticare
							utm
							enterprise
							atp

					fortiflex	
						an annually contracted program to create multiple sets of a single entitlement per vm
						entitlements contain a vm base and forticare bundle
						four support service bundle types are available
							only forticare
							utm
							enterprise
							atp

				vcpu number upgrade during contracted term
					on v-series and normal series we have no these options

					s-series
						supported 
						you can also upgrade the support service bundle
						contact a fortinet sales representative to upgrade

					fortiflex
						supported
						you can apply different vm entitlement configurations in the fortiflex portal
						api is not supported at this time

				vcpu number downgrade during contracted term
					on v-series and normal series we have no these options

				vdom support
					v-series and normal
						by default, each cpu by default, all cpu level supports up to a levels do not support adding vdoms
						v-series
							certain number of vdoms
							refer to the fortigate-vm data sheet for default limits

					s-series
						by default, all cpu levels do not support adding vdoms
						s-series vm instances support the subscription vdom license

			adding vdoms with fortigate v-series
				each fortigate-vm base license type allows a default number of virtual domains (vdom)

				activate the fortigate-vm with the base license
				add more vdoms to the fortigate-vm

				to activate the fortigate-vm with the base license
					1. purchase and register the fortigate-vm base license in forticare
						purchase the fortigate-vm base license from fortinet or a fortinet reseller
						
						you receive a license certification with a registration code
							open the certification 
							log in to fortinet customer service & support
						
							go to register now and enter the provided registration code
							
							follow the registration process
								the serial number generates and displays on the registration completion page
						
						go to asset > manage/view products
							click the serial number to download the license file

					2. upload the fortigate-vm base license file to fortios
						log in to the fortigate-vm gui
						in dashboard > status
							in the virtual machine widget, click fortigate vm license click the upload button
							
							select the fortigate-vm base license file, then click ok. the fortigate-vm reboots after applying the base license

					3. verify the fortigate-vm base license status and vdom information
						log in to the fortigate-vm gui
						
						in dashboard > status
							in the virtual machine widget, ensure that there is a checkmark in front of the fortigate-vm base license name 
							the checkmark indicates that the base license is valid

						you can check vdom information using the cli
						the following output shows that the maximum number of vdoms is currently one. this is correct since the fortigate-vm base license only supports the default root vdom that the system uses

					to add more vdoms to the fortigate-vm
						purchase and register the fortigate-vm upgrade license in forticare. this example adds 15 vdoms
							purchase the fortigate-vm upgrade license from fortinet or a fortinet reseller
							
							you receive a license certification with a registration code
							open the certification

							log in to fortinet customer service & support
							
							go to asset > register/activate and enter the provided registration code

							on the specify license confirmation information screen, enter the fortigate-vm serial number to apply the vdom upgrade license to the fortigate-vm

							follow the registration process

							go to asset > manage/view products >
								select the desired product, then click license & key. the vdom upgrade license displays under registered license(s), and a key for adding 15 vdoms 

						2. apply the fortigate-vm upgrade license key to fortios
							log in to the fortigate-vm cli in the local console or using ssh
							
							apply the vdom upgrade license key
								execute upd-vd-license m6jsd-8ee32-vhijb-n
								#update vdom license succeeded
						
						3. verify the fortigate-vm vdom information
							log in to the fortigate-vm cli in the local console or using ssh
							
							check vdom information using the cli
							the following output shows that the maximum number of vdoms is currently 15
							
							when you add vdoms for the first time on a fortigate-vm v-series instance, fortios does not count the default vdom, as the default vdom is the so-called root vdom that the system uses and fortios does not treat it as a countable vdom in terms of vdom addition

							therefore, as in this example, if your fortigate-vm had the default vdom configuration, then you add 15 vdoms, fortios displays the maximum  vdom number as 15, not 16

						get system status
						#max number of virtual domains: 1

			terraform
				fortinet's terraform support provides customers with more ways to efficiently deploy, manage, and automate security  across physical fortigate appliances and virtual environments

				you can use terraform to automate various it infrastructure needs, thereby diminishing mistakes from repetitive manual configurations

				if fortinet is releasing a new fortios version, your organization may require you to test a new functionality to determine how it may impact the environment before globally deploying the new version

				rapidly stand up environments and test these functions prior to production environment integration provides a resource-efficient and fault-tolerant approach

			pf and vf sr-iov driver and virtual spu support
				physical function (pf) and virtual function (vf) pci passthrough and sr-iov drivers in fortigate guest vm are supported
					**single root i/o virtualization, how assign nic to vms
						physical function
							port1 assigned as dedicated interafce from server to this machine

						virtual function
							one port will be assigned to many vms

					**pci passthrough means bypass all virtualization engiens i/o features(esxi and kvm) , direcct negotiate with interfaces
					**if used live-migration and resource sharing , passthrough might effect on these
					**dpdk > some library on linux could help us on packet forwarding (no perform on esxi and nsx-t over vmxnet3 (no vspu))

				pf provides the ability for pci passthrough, but requires an entire network interface card (nic) for a vm
				it can usually achieve greater performance than a virtual function (vf) based sr-iov

				pf is also expensive
				while vf allows one nic to be shared among multiple guests vms, pf is allocated to one port on a vm

				other hypervisors, such as xen or microsoft hyper-v, may work with vspu, although they are unverified

				all tools and software utilities for uefi 1.x have been removed from 6.2.0 and later releases
				update to uefi 2.x to use the uefi tools or software utilities

				You perform the configuration to use PF or VF on the hypervisor, and do not configure it on the FortiGate
					diagnose hardware deviceinfo nic port2
					Name:port2
					Driver:i40e
					Version:2.4.10

	getting start
		low encryption models
			some fortigate models support a low encryption (lenc) license
			with an lenc license, fortigate devices are considered low encryption models and are identified by lenc, for example fg-100e-lenc
			
			lenc models cannot use or inspect high encryption protocols, such as 3des and aes
			lenc models only use 56-bit des encryption to work with ssl vpn and ipsec vpn, and they are unable to perform ssl inspection

			after upload license on device , bios and startup setting wil be updated to lower levels

			with an lenc license, fortigate devices are considered low encryption models and are identified by lenc, fg-100e-lenc
			
			with an lenc license applied to fortigate, fortios boots up with bios security level 0, but after applying a crypto license to upgrade to a high encryption model, the fortigate boots up with bios security level 2

			bios-level signature and file integrity checking for more information
			
			lenc models cannot use or inspect high encryption protocols, such as 3des and aes
			lenc models only use 56-bit des encryption to work with ssl vpn and ipsec vpn, and they are unable to perform ssl inspection

			**except general available could used lenc

		loading artifacts from a cdn
			to improve gui performance, loading static gui artifacts cached in cdn (content delivery network) servers closer to the user instead of the fortigate can be enabled

			this allows the gui to load more quickly with less latency for administrators who are accessing the fortigate remotely
			upon failure, the files fall back to loading from the fortigate

			the cdn is only used after successful administrator logins

				config system global
				set gui-cdn-usage enable
				end

			Tab
				Ctrl + V then Tab

			Space (as part of a string value, not to end the string)
				Enclose the string in single or double quotation marks: "Security Administrator" or 'Security Administrator'
				Precede the space with a backslash: Security\ Administrator.

			" or ' (as part of a string value, not to begin or end the string)
				\' or \"

				don't check case sensetives
