			-n print line number with output lines
				sh sys interface | grep -n port3
				15: edit "port3"

			-f print fortinet config context
				sh sys interface | grep -f port3
				edit "port3"
				set vdom "root"
				set ip 192.168.101.1 255.255.255.0
				set allowaccess ping
				set type physical
				set alias "LAN" 
				set snmp-index 3
				next
				end

			-v select non-matching lines
				sh sys interface | grep -f port3

				**show all interafces setting except port3

			-c only print count of matching lines
			-A print num lines of trailing context
				sh system interface | grep -A 2 port3
				set vdom root
				set ip 192.168.101.1 255.255.255.0

			-b print num lines of leading context
				sh system interface | grep -B 6 port3
				next
				edit "port2"

				set type physicalsi
				set snmp-index 2
				next
				edit "port3"

		screen paging
			by default, the cli will pause after displaying each page worth of text when a command has multiple pages of output
			this can be useful when viewing lengthy outputs that might exceed the buffer of terminal emulator
			
			when the display pauses and shows -- more -- , you can
				press enter to show the next line
				press q to stop showing results and return to the command prompt
				press an arrow key, insert, home, delete, end, page up, or page down to show the next few pages
				press any other key to show the next page
				wait for about 30 seconds for the console to truncate the output and return to the command prompt
			
			when pausing the screen is disabled, press ctrl + c to stop the output and log out of the fortigate
	
			to disable pausing the cli output
				config system console
				set output standard

				end

				**on gui > right top > admin > config > revision
					on this pannel we have revert (like snashot) , diff (show difference between this revision and running configs) 
					**if create revision then revert on old versions , could see our new vesion on flash

					you can manage multiple versions of configuration files on models that have a 512mb flash memory and higher

					revision control requires either a configured central management server or the local hard drive, if your fortigate has this feature

					typically, configuration backup to local drive is not available on lower-end models
					
					the central management server can either be a fortimanager unit or fortigate cloud
					
					if central management is not configured on your fortigate unit, a message appears instructing you to either 
						enable central management
						obtain a valid license

					when revision control is enabled on your fortigate unit, and configuration backups have been made, a list of saved revisions of those backed-up configurations appears

				trouble
					configuration file error
						this error occurs when attempting to upload a configuration file that is incompatible with the device
						this may be due to the configuration file being for a different model or being saved from a different version of firmware
						
						upload a configuration file that is for the correct model of fortigate device and the correct version of the firmware


					invalid password
						when the configuration file is saved, it can be protected by a password
						the password entered during the upload process is not matching the one associated with the configuration file
						
						use the correct password if the file is password protected

		led
			major is os crash ....
			minor is  hightemp or bad behave

			logo
				green
					unit is on

				blue
					fortiwifi is on

				off
					device is off

			power (pwr)
				green
					the unit is on and/or both power supplies are functioning
				
				amber
					one power supply is functioning
				
				flashing amber
					power supply failure
				
				red
					the unit is on, but only one power supply is functional
				
				flashing red
					power failure
				
				off
					the unit is off

			status (sta)
				green
					normal
				
				flashing green
					booting up
				
				amber
					major or minor alarm
				
				flashing amber
					ble is on
				
				red
					major alarm
				
				flashing red
					ble is on (pairinig state)
				
				off
					the unit is off

			bypass (byp)
				amber
					bypass port pair is active (fail to open state)
				
				off
					bypass port pair is off (fail to close)

			alarm
				red
					major alarm
				
				amber
					minor alarm
				
				off
					no alarms

			ha
				green
					operating in an ha cluster
				
				amber or red
					ha failover
				
				off
					ha disabled

			max poe
				green, amber, or red
					maximum poe power allocated
					*amber is higher than normal watts usuage
					*red is we can not  deliver power but conencted

				off
					poe power available or normal

			poe
				green
					power delivered
				
				flashing green
					error or poe device requesting power

				off
					no poe device connected or no power delivered

			svc
				green
					svc is on
				
				flashing green
					svc activity
				
				off
					svc is off

			3g/4g
				green
					3g / 4g service is on

				flashing green
					3g / 4g activity

				off
					3g / 4g service is off
			
			wifi
				green
					wifi connected

				flashing green
					wifi activity

				off
					wifi is off

			power supply
				green
					power supply operating normally

				flashing green
					power detected, but power supply not providing power or is in standby mode

				amber
					power output is off, there is a power supply error, or there is no input power but the redundant supply is on

				flashing amber
					power supply error or warning events, or the power supply should be replaced

				red
					cord unplugged or power lost

				flashing red
					power supply warning events

				off
					power not detected

			power supply ok
				green
					standby rail and main output on

				flashing green
					standby rail and main output off

				off
					error or no ac power input

			power supply fail
				amber
					main output or fan error detected

				flashing amber
					power supply warning event detected

				off
					no errors or no power

			power supply input
				green
					input voltage within normal range

				flashing green
					over or under voltage warning

				off
					no input power

			power supply output
				green
					output voltage normal

				flashing green
					standby mode

				amber
					critical error

				flashing amber
					warning

				off
					no output

			fan
				green
					fan(s) operating normally
				
				flashing green 
					fan switching/initialization in progress
				
				amber 
					fan failure
				
				red fan
					error, rpm too low or too high, or both fan sets have at least one alert
				
				flashing red
					one fan set has at least one alert
				
				off
					fan error or fan is off

			local log
				disk 
					define local log storage on the fortigate
						some scenarios need 2 disk 
							one of them used for log (must format that) , not used for raid or ...
							if used on security fabric with same concept fgt-1 and fgt-2 which has 2 disk (one of them used for log), on slave in security fabric could set override setup for using logs on local disk
								**better used fabric config

								**better  used root fabric device fgt-1 with 2 ssd or more storage on it (used local disk + faz)
								**downstream devics with 1 ssd is okay
					
					enable
						logs will be stored on a local disk
						local disk logging is not available in the gui if the security fabric is enabled
						when the security fabric is enabled, disk logging can still be configured on the root fortigate in the cli but is not available for downstream fortigates
					
					disable
						logs will be stored remotely to fortianalyzer/fortimanager or to a cloud logging device

					**logs on-permis or cloud
						realtime upload
							if sued fortigate cloud must used this model
							we have no store and upload feature on 
						store and upload  mechanism
							cache some  part of logs on local disk then send to faz
							faz cloud used this mechanism
				
				enable local reports 
					define log reporting on the fortigate
					
					enable
						local reports will be available on the fortigate
						reports can be reviewed in log & report > local reports
						if the security fabric is enabled, local reports can be enabled in system > feature visibility
						
					disable
						local reports will not be available on the fortigate

				enable historical fortiview
					define the presentation of log information on fortiview
					
					enable
						historical log data will be available on a fortiview monitor by default, logs older than seven days are deleted
						disk logging must be enabled

					disable
						historical log data will not be available on fortiview	

			remote logging and archiving
				send logs to fortianalyzer/fortimanager
					define the status of remote logging to fortianalyzer and fortimanager:
							enable
								logs are sent to fortianalyzer or fortimanager for remote logging
								http transaction logs are also sent to a fortianalyzer unit to generate additional details in reports

							disable
								logs are stored to system memory, a local disk, or a cloud logging device
			
					server set the server ip address for the fortianalyzer or fortimanager. use test connectivity to test the connection status to the server. 

					connection status displays authorization status on fortianalyzer:	
						 successful
						 	the fortigate is connected to the fortianalyzer remote logging to the fortianalyzer can be configured
							disk logging must be enabled for logs to be stored locally on the fortigate by default, logs older than seven days are deleted from the disk log age can be configured in the cli approximately 75% of disk space is available for log storage 
								diagnose sys logdisk usage 

				config log fortianalyzer setting
				set upload-option store-and-upload
				#realtime is another option that just forward to fortianalyzer , if enable disk logging means local disk will write the data
				end

			upload option 
				select the frequency of log uploads to the remote device
					real time
						logs are sent to the remote device in real time

					every minute
						logs are sent to the remote device once every minute
						this option is unavailable if the security fabric connections is enabled
			
				every 5 minutes
					logs are sent to the remote device once every five minutes
					this is the default option. this option is unavailable if the security fabric connection is enabled

				store-and-upload
					store logs to a local disk before uploading to fortianalyzer or fortimanager at a scheduled time
					**after upload delete all cached and temporal content

				**if disk get full behave like fifo remove and used 75% disk for logs

			uuids in traffic log
				policy 
					define the use of policy uuids in traffic logs
						 enable
						 	policy uuids are stored in traffic logs. uuids can be matched for each source and destination that match a policy in the traffic log

						 disable
						 	policy uuids are excluded from the traffic logs

				address 
					define the use of address uuids in traffic logs
						enable
							address uuids are stored in traffic logs. when viewing forward traffic logs, a filter is automatically set based on uuid

						disable
							address uuids are excluded from traffic logs

			log settings
				event logging 
					define the allowed set of event logs to be recorded
					
					all
						all event logs will be recorded

					customize
						select specific event log types to be recorded

				local traffic log
					define the allowed set of traffic logs to be recorded

					all
						all traffic logs to and from the fortigate will be recorded

					customize
						select specific traffic logs to be recorded. deselect all options to disable traffic logging

					local traffic logging is disabled by default due to the high volume of logs generated

			the fortigate can store logs locally to its system memory or a local disk
				logs can also be stored externally on a storage device, such as fortianalyzer, fortianalyzer cloud, fortigate cloud, or a syslog server
				premium (afac) () and standard (fazc) () subscriptions

			disk logging must be enabled for logs to be stored locally on the fortigate by default, logs older than seven days are deleted from the disk
			log age can be configured in the cli
			approximately 75% of disk space is available for log storage
				diagnose sys logdisk  usuage
				diagnose sys logdisk  quota
				diagnose sys logdisk  smart
				#health
				
				diagnose sys logdisk  status
				
				after analyze disk change percentage
					config log disk setting
					set status enable
					set ips-archive enable
					set max-policy-packet-capture-size 100
					#if packet capture reach 100 mb start new file 

					set log-quota 0
					#if your disk  were 1tb we can set 50 as 500 gig of hdd then our limits and ..at the bottom will be worked on this 500gig

					set dlp-archive-quota 0
					set report-quota  0
					#0 means unlimited

					set max imum- log-age 7
					#day , after this roll logs and forward to faz or ...  (.gz on fortigate)

					set upload disable
					#for roll and forward to faz must enable this, if disable and reach 7 days , all logs delete

					set full-first-warning-threshold 75
					#if reach 75% send alerts , roll  the logs

					set full-second-warning-threshold 90
					set full-final-warning-threshold 95
					#we have no overwrite, if reach 100% overwrite happen or no log happen

					set max-log-file-size 20
					#mb

					set roll-schedule daily
					set roll-time 00:00
					set diskfull overwrite
					#or nolog option

					end

			Log filters
				log filter settings can be configured to determine which logs are recorded to the fortianalyzer, fortimanager, and syslog servers
				this allows certain logging levels and types of logs to be directed to specific log devices

		log buffer on on fortigate ssd disk
			when read miglogd , see children term
				when negotiate with fortianalyzer , if lost conenction our fortigate must cache all logs on memory and disk to forward on faz later

				here we can change the buffer values
					config system global
					set faz-disk-buffer-size 200
					#default is 100mb

					end

					**if change this miglod  will be reset and change behavior
						the queued logs are buffered to the memory first and then disk
						main miglogd handles the disk buffering job, while miglogd-children handles the memory buffering
						disk buffer statistics only appear under main-miglogd, and memory buffer statistics only appears under miglogd-children
						if the total buffer is full, new logs will overwrite the old logs

						**if during cache  logs over 200mb coul not conenct to faz , all logs will be fifo mechanism and delete all old devices
							config log disk setting
							set diskfull nolog
							end

							**better set log qouta on 200 mb then ages change on lower values

			snmp oid for logs that failed to send
				when a syslog server encounters low-performance conditions and slows down to respond, the buffered syslog messages in the kernel might overflow after a certain number of retransmissions, causing the overflowed messages to be lost
				oids track the lost messages or failed logs (used sequence numebr to prevent lost or ..)

				on snmp setup , disk values must  selected to alert as full states or monitro state

	troubleshooting
		get system status
		#firmware versions and fortiguard engine versions, and other system information

		get system performance status 
		#cpu and memory states, average network usage, average sessions and session setup rate, viruses caught, ips attacks blocked, and uptime
			when we have some process with  low priority , these type most release cpu and resources, on linux we have application which help us to process these type procedures with lower priority called nice which schedule or scheduler background process with best managers , on 100% usuage means overload on reosurces

			iowait ,  write data on disk and network connectivities , cpu idle  time and signaling (delay and idle time) , if were to much means we have network trouble
			
			irq or interupts on hardware(crash on os with reboot) and software (os or kernel or process) 

			softirq (used npu and  accelerators) , ips , system call ,vpn , firewall , schedule timmer , if were high means some process get loop or trouble

			**CPU states: 0% user 0% system 0% nice 100% idle 0% iowait 0% irq 0% softirq
				if see this state, no response state , reboot system
					motherboard  , cpu ...trouble
					kernel panic
					force shutdown with  electeric pulses
					overload reosurces and  too many proxy policies

					**high average network usage may indicate high traffic processing on the fortigate, a very low or zero, average session setup rate may indicate the proxy is overloaded and unable to do its job

			**CPU states: 1% user 98% system 0% nice 1% idle
				shows that all of the cpu is being used by system processes, and the fortigate is overloaded
				when overloading occurs, it is possible a process such as scanunitid is using all the resources to scan traffic
				in this case you need to reduce the amount of traffic being scanned by blocking unwanted protocols, configuring more security policies to limit scanning to certain protocols, or similar actions

				it is also possible a hacker has accessed your network and is overloading it with malicious activity, such as running a spam server or using zombie pcs to attack other networks on the internet

				**ddos or spu bypass and process by main cpu

				get system performance firewall statistics

				get system performance top
					shows all of the top processes that are running on the fortigate and their cpu usage
					the process names are on the left
					if a process is using most of the cpu cycles, investigate it to determine whether the activity is normal

		**the session setup rate is a global command. if you have multiple vdoms configured with many sessions in each vdom, the session setup rate per vdom will be slower than if there are no vdoms configured

		diagnose hardware sysinfo cpu
		#processor name and generation , microcode firware and patch on cpu
			fpu (floating point )used for math and  rating
			cache size l2
			tlb size show us cpu  how navigate on memory and addressing on memory (4k pages)
			cache line flush size (clflush ) memory optimization and how  read and write
			flags
				pse36 is memory addressing 36 bit as extension
				clflush  is memory optimization
				pat is page attribute table and memory access  also accelerate read and writes
				sse2  / sse4a is acceleration and encryption , inspection
				popcnt is population count  used for cidr and networking
				api is application programmable intracontroller used for irq and inter controller management
				nx is not execute bit used for malware control while cache on disk and scan them
				lm used for 64bit os 
				svm used  for passthrough vm on server 
				tsc is time stamp counter used for log and event timing
				cmov is conditional moving used for firewall polices and rules
				system cal support 

			cpu intensive feature
				vpn high-level encryption
				intensive scanning of all traffic
				logging all traffic and packets
				dashboard widgets that frequently perform data updates

				diagnose sys top-all
				#m > sort by mem , p > sort by process , q is left the schema

					0U, 0N, 0S, 100I, 0WA, 0HI, 0SI, 0ST; 3040T, 2437F
					bcm.user 93 S < 3.1 0.4

					-------------------------
					u percentage of user space applications that are currently using the cpu

					n percentage of time that the cpu spent on low priority processes since the last shutdown
					
					s percentage of system processes (or kernel processes) that are using the cpu

					i percentage of idle cpu resources
					
					wa percentage of time that the cpu spent waiting on io peripherals since the last shutdown
					
					hi percentage of time that the cpu spent handling hardware interrupt routines since the last shutdown (irq)
					
					si percentage of time that the cpu spent handling software interrupt routines since the last shutdown
					
					st steal time percentage of time a virtual cpu waits for the physical cpu when the hypervisor is servicing another virtual processor
					
					t total fortios system memory in mb
					
					f free memory in mb
					----------------------------
					newcli the process (or thread) name duplicate process or thread names indicate that separate instances of that process or thread are running

					20195 the process or thread id, which can be any number

					r current state of the process or thread. the process or thread state can be
						r  running
						s  sleep
						z  zombie (detect no cleanup)
						d disk sleep (iowait)
					
					0.1 the percentage of cpu capacity that the process or thread is using cpu usage can range from 0.0 for a process or thread that is sleeping to higher values for a process or thread that's taking a lot of cpu time
					
					0.1 the amount of memory that the process or thread is using memory usage can range from 0.1 to 5.5 and higher

				diagnose sys top 5 20
				#5 is delay and 20 is number of process will be shown

		get hardware memory 
		diagnose hardware sysinfo memory
		#information about memory , betre see memfree on 20% (if be 10% means conserve mode or freezing)
			active (anoun) means vpn and sessions except  fortigate loging ..
			swap better be on 0 or near 0 cause effect on main memory
			sreclaimable, sunreclaim some process on kernel could be swap between memory and disk
			huge page used for  memory optimization
			commited_as or limit used for memory request  and eaach process usuage , if were more than total memory  values , cause conserver 
			shmem means shared memory which worked between process
			direct map 2m or 4k , used  for mappning toward memory

			high memory usage
				as with any system, a fortigate has limited hardware resources, such as memory, and all processes running on the fortigate share the memory
				each process uses more or less memory, depending on its workload. for example, a process usually uses more memory in high traffic situations
				if some processes use all of the available memory, other processes will not be able to run

				when high memory usage occurs, the services may freeze up, connections may be lost, or new connections may be refused

				if you see high memory usage in the memory widget, the fotigate may be handling high traffic volumes

				alternatively, the fortigate may have problems with connection pool limits that are affecting a single proxy
				if the fortigate receives large volumes of traffic on a specific proxy, the unit may exceed the connection pool limit
				if the number of free connections within a proxy connection pool reaches zero, issues may occur

		session management
			config system global
			set tcp-halfclose-timer 30
			#default is 120 seconds , if one sde close  you should close it 30 seconds later

			set tcp-halfopen-timer 30
			#synflood attack , half open syn connections 

			set tcp-timewait-timer 0
			#two side close conenction ,  better set 0 and freeup memory

			set udp-idle-timer 60
			end

				firmware build version (use the get system status command)
				network topology diagram
				recent configuration file
				recent debug log (optional)

			do not provide the output from the execute tac report unless the support team requests it
			the output from this command is very large and is not required in many cases

		simcard modem and ppp connection on device

			#com, detect , history (tracking) , external-modem (vable base) , query (signaling state, reset

			#usb base connection

			#modem's configuration, vendor and custom product identification number

		sniffer
			before you start sniffing packets, you should prepare to capture the output to a file
			a large amount of data may scroll by and you will not be able to see it without saving it first
			once the packet sniffing count is reached, you can end the session and analyze the output in the file
			
			diagnose sniffer packet <interface_name> <'filter'> <verbose> <count> <tsformat>
				verbose
					1 print header of packets (basic mode)
					2 print header and data from ip of packets (payload will  be checked as dpi)
					3 print header and data from ethernet of packets (mac address)
					4 print header of packets with interface name (interface)

					if we have no filter must set none term to capture all packets

				tsfoormat
					l means local
					a means utc

					**fortiasic np4 or np6 interface pairs that offload traffic will change the packet flow
					before debugging any np4 or np6 interfaces, disable offloading on those interfaces
						diagnose npu <interface pair> fastpath disable

						**where interface pair isnp4, np6, np4lite, ornp6lite

					**if fortigate is connected to fortianalyzer or forticloud, the diagnose debug flow output will be recorded as event log messages and then sent to the devices
					do not run this command longer than necessary, as it generates a significant amount of data

		hardware  nic

				physical as duplex and speed

				bottle neck checking with buffer overflow might be on this part

		xaui link
			used for interconnectivity between npu and ...

			diagnose npu np6 xaui-hash port1 6 1.1.1.1 2.2.2.1 4567 80
			#38xxd, 39xxd, 34xxe, 36xxe, and 5001e series devices , show us data stream between npus

		arp
			update 5 min 

			get system arp
			#used means how may times used this , state means dynamic / incomplete / verify , confirm with fortigate (sysnc ip and mac)
				state
					the fortigate must make an arp request when it tries to reach a new destination
					the base arp reachable value determines how often an arp request it sent; the default is 30 seconds
					the actual arp reachable time is a random number between half and three halves of the base reachable time, or 15 to 45 seconds. the random number is updated every five minutes

					000000002 or 0x02 reachable an arp response was received

					000000004 or 0x04 stale no arp response within the expected time

					000000008 or 0x08 delay a transition state between stale and reachable before probes are sent out
					
					000000020 or 0x20 failed did not manage to resolve within the maximum configured number of probes
					
					000000040 or 0x40 noarp device does not support arp, ipsec interface

					000000080 or 0x80 permanent a statically defined arp entry

					conf system global
					st arp-max-entry  10
					#10 arp could be used 
					end

			diagnose ip arp list
			diagnose ip arp add
			diagnose ip arp delete port1 192.168.101.200

			execute clear system arp table

			arp reachable tiem on interface
				config sys interface
				edit port1
				set reachable-time 30000
				#max 3600000 milisec
				end

			static arp
				config system arp-table
				edit 1
				set interface port1
				set ip 192.168.101.200
				set mac bc:14:01:e9:77:02
				end

			diagnose sys device list root
			#max depth  means recursive lookup on rib  if were 1  is okay if were 5 means heacy loop and routing , table size means how many routes will be managed
				main tabel means how many  fortigates rib  updated? version 0x11 means 17 update (route update to reach destinations)
				rpdb (route policy data base)
				local table changes  entries and local routes 

		fortiguard
			these must be reachable
				execute ping service.fortiguard.net
				execute ping update.fortiguard.net

			on ha must used same license level

			diagnose debug rating
			get webfilter status
			#check rtt and ... all values which connected to fortiguard (each 2min)

			flags
				d 
					the server was found through the dns lookup of the hostname
					if the hostname returns more than one ip address, all of them are flagged with d and are used first for init requests before falling back to the other servers

				i 
					the server to which the last init request was sent
				
				f 
					the server hasn't responded to requests and is considered to have failed
				
				t 
					the server is currently being timed
				
				s 
					rating requests can be sent to the server
					the flag is set for a server only 
						the server exists in the servers list received from the fortimanager or any other init server
						the server list received from the fortimanager is empty so the fortimanager is the only server that the fortigate knows and it should be used as the rating server

			to access FortiGuard servers
				sorting server list by weight and smallest rtt
					(fortiguard time - local time ) * 10 > base weight

					if were success connection x*1
					if were fail connection y*10
						for each server on list we calculate these 
						then (x*1) - (y*10) = z

						if server near 50 means used for update

		tcp socks
			diagnose sys tcpsocks
			#show all opened ports, same as local-in policies
				rma and wma means read and write from buffer
				fma means flags and bit masks
				tma  means timeout base milisec
				socket type 
					1-2 tcp and udp
					4

				err
					111 connection fail
	**************************************
	hardware acceleration part2
			from np7 and later we have better process and more throughput
				fortigates with np7 processors and hyperscale firewall features (fortigate-1800f, fortigate-1801f, fortigate-2600f, fortigate-2601f, fortigate-3500f, fortigate-3501f, fortigate-4200f, fortigate-4201f, fortigate-4400f, and fortigate-4401f)

			np6 standard processor (40gbps throughput)
				doesn't support ssl inspection , aes and gcm
				highend

			np6xlite enhanced features (36gbps supports aes-gcm)
				midlevel

			np6lite lightweight version (10gbps fewer features)
				used main cpu to manage another components which could not process on np
				entry level
				no dpi , main process on cpu

			faster throughput offloads traffic from cpu to dedicated chips
			lower latency direct packet processing in hardware
			better scalability handles millions of sessions efficiently

			step-by-step flow
			   first packet goes to cpu for policy check
			   fast-path check if eligible session is offloaded to np
			   subsequent packets processed directly by np without cpu involvement

			fast-path requirements
			   for a session to be offloaded
			   layer 2 must be ipv4 (0x0800) or ipv6 (0x86dd)
			   layer 3 ipv4 or ipv6 traffic
			   layer 4 tcp udp icmp or sctp
			   
			   *cannot offload proxy-based inspections (eg web filtering dlp)

			offloads by np6 processors
			   ipsec vpn  
			      aes 3des sha-2 (np6xlite adds aes-gcm)
			   
			   traffic shaping   
			      np6xlite provides full stats others offload but dont log drops
			   
			   anomaly checks 
			      drops malformed packets before cpu sees them
			   
			   multicast   
			      including multicast over ipsec
			   
			   link aggregation (lag)
			      distributes sessions across multiple nps
			   
			   inter-vdom links
			      except emac vlan interfaces


			config system npu
			   set fastpath enable          
			      #enable np offloading

			   set dedicated-management-cpu enable  
			      #reserve cpu0 for management

			   set capwap-offload enable
			      #offload capwap (if supported)
			end

			config system npu
			   set ipsec-asic-offload enable  
			      #offload diffie-hellman

			   set ipsec-inbound-cache disable  
			      #fix anti-reply issues

			   set uesp-offload enable       
			      #offload esp-over-udp (port 4500)
			end

			config system npu
			   set intf-shaping-offload enable  
			      #enable hardware shaping

			   set qos-mode priority           
			      #prioritize high-cos traffic
			end


			verifying & troubleshooting offloading
			   checking if traffic is offloaded
			      diagnose sys session list
			         #look for npu_info field (offloaded) or no_ofld_reason (not offloaded)

			      diag sniffer packet port1 'host 1.1.1.1'  

			      diagnose npu np6 session-stats 0  
			         #shows offloaded sessions per np
			      
			      diagnose npu np6 ipsec-stats      
			         #ipsec offload status

			problem & solutions
			tcp sessions failing under load  
			   enable delay-tcp-npu-session in firewall policy (on session search engine (sse))
			   	used cache list lookup over cpu and npu (while tcp session drop)

			ipsec anti-reply drops 
			   disable ipsec-inbound-cache
			   #on lazy networks  we have cache feature help us to provide faster negotiate , on ipsec delay is important and long delay could not used on sequence numbers , so disable cache help us to work with delays (might some hackers try  to work on your ipsec)

			session drift (orphaned sessions)   
			   run diagnose npu np6 sse-purge-drift 0
			   #we have some zombies , cpu and npu get confilict on alive session or drop them so zombies will work on network to cause harm
			   #0 means no delay and clear from one side

			xaui link congestion balance traffic across interfaces on different xaui ports
				config system npu
				config xaui-loadbalance
				#here set load balancing ports
				end

			*advanced optimization 
			   a balancing traffic across nps
			   use link aggregation (lag) to distribute sessions

			   assign interfaces to round robin groups (for high throughput flows)

			   host protection engine (hpe) tuning
			      prevents cpu overload from ddos attacks (copp cisco)

			      config system np6
			         edit np6_0
			            config hpe
			               set tcpsyn-max 600000   
			                  #max syn packets/sec

			               set udp-max 200000      
			                  #max udp packets/sec

			               set enable-shaper enable
			            end
			         end

			   multicast & high-bandwidth flows
			      config system npu
			         set mcast-session-accounting session-based  
			            #better for many sessions

			         set lag-out-port-select enable  
			            #optimize lag egress traffic
			      end

			*using diagnose npu np6 hpe to monitor ddos protection

			fortigate 300e/400e  single np6 16x1g ports
			fortigate 1500d   dual np6 isf for cross-np offload
			fortigate 3960e/3980e   multiple np6s supports round robin hashing
			fortigate 1000d   np direct (no isf must use same np for ingress/egress)

			what is isf
			   the integrated switch fabric (isf) is a high-speed internal switching system that connects
			      multiple np6 processors
			      all data interfaces
			      cpu management plane

			      like vlan in switch this is logical and ports just use one direct lane on mother board to connect npu

			      flexible traffic flow any-to-any offloading (traffic can enter one np and exit another)
			      load balancing distributes sessions across all available nps
			      simplified design no need to manually match ingress/egress interfaces

			   fortigate 1500d
			   fortigate 3200d
			   fortigate 5001d

			   get hardware npu np6 port-list  
			      #cross-chip offloading yes (means use isf)

			what is np direct
			   a minimalist architecture where
			      no isf exists between np6 processors
			      direct interface-to-np mapping

			      ultra-low latency bypasses isf (saves ~1-2 microseconds per packet)
			      critical requirement traffic must enter and exit the same np6

			   fortigate 1000d
			   fortigate 2000e/2500e
			   fortigate 3700d (ports 25-32 in low-latency mode)


			   config system np6  
			      edit np6_0  
			         set low-latency-mode enable  
			            #bypasses isf  
			   end  

			                        isf                                 np direct
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			latency                 higher (~2μs added)                 lowest possible
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			traffic flexibility     any-to-any offload                  must use same np for in/out
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			best for                general-purpose deployments         high-frequency trading, hpc
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			lag support             can mix interfaces from all nps     all lag members must use same np
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

			check port-to-np mapping first
			   diagnose npu np6 port-list  
			      #ingress/egress ports are on same np  

			never mix nps in lags
			   config system interface  
			      edit agg1  
			      set type aggregate  
			      set member port1 port2  
			         #must be same np  
			   end  

			use low-latency mode explicitly
			   config system np6  
			      edit np6_0  
			      set low-latency-mode enable  
			   end

			symptom traffic not offloading in np direct mode
			   root cause mismatched ingress/egress nps
			   
			   diagnose npu np6 port-list | grep portx  
			      #verify np assignment  
			      #reconfigure policies to use same-np interfaces  

			symptom high latency despite np direct
			   root cause isf accidentally used (low-latency mode disabled)

			   config system np6  
			      edit np6_0  
			      set low-latency-mode enable  
			   end

			hybrid approach on fortigate 3700d
			   ports 1-24 isf mode (flexible offload)
			   ports 25-32 np direct (low-latency)

			   config system np6  
			      edit np6_0  
			         set low-latency-mode disable  
			            #isf for first np  
			      next  
			      edit np6_1  
			         set low-latency-mode enable   
			            #np direct for second np  
			   end  

			*always verify with diagnose npu np6 port-list 

			if np-direct get fail on npu or npu were unreachable process by cpu

			better do these
			   monitor np usage with diagnose npu np6 session-stats
			   use lags to distribute traffic across multiple nps
			   enable per-session-accounting if logging is needed

			should not to do
			   enable check-protocol-header strict (disables all acceleration)(layer 4 (header length , ip header , spi , checksum))(if strict worked on main cpu)

			   mix proxy and flow-based policies in the same rule (breaks offloading)
